#5108. Crypto-friendly Robinhood brings US IPO access to UK retail investors

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Robinhood's IPO Access service, launched in the U.S. in 2021, lets UK customers request allocations at the official IPO price.

Robinhood Robinhood Markets, Inc. (HOOD) has launched its IPO Access service in the UK, letting retail investors request shares in new U.S. listings at the official offering price before public trading begins.

UK customers can submit requests through their general investment accounts, Robinhood said in a statement on Monday. The app includes the prospectus and SEC filings for each available listing, which investors can read before deciding whether to request shares. Robinhood said it is working with a UK Public Offer Platform provider to provide access to eligible initial public offerings.

Robinhood first introduced IPO Access in the U.S. in 2021. The company said it has since worked on more than 50 IPOs, making the service available to millions of U.S. customers.

"As we bring IPO Access to the UK, we want to support that same opportunity for Britain's retail investors: access to the next generation of innovative public companies," Robinhood UK President Jordan Sinclair said in the statement.

The launch follows Robinhood's August rollout of crypto trading to eligible UK customers through Bitstamp, the exchange it acquired for $200 million last year. The service allows users to buy and sell more than 50 cryptocurrencies directly within its main UK investing app, alongside stocks, ISAs, options, and futures.

Robinhood has also expanded its digital asset business beyond cryptocurrency trading. In July, the company launched Robinhood Chain on mainnet, alongside 24/7 trading for eligible users of its tokenized stock products. At the time of its UK crypto launch, a spokesperson told The Block that Robinhood was also exploring bringing products such as Stock Tokens to its UK app.

IPO allocation requests will appear alongside customers' portfolios in their general investment accounts. Robinhood said it does not charge commission fees for the service, but warned that investing in IPOs involves significant risk and that share prices can be highly volatile, particularly shortly after listing.

#5107. North Korea Is Minting Its Own Meme Coins to Launder Binance Hack Loot

Elliptic says North Korean launderers created six throwaway meme tokens on NEAR between Nov. 5 and 7, moving about $430,000 of stolen Binance funds through homemade liquidity pools.

North Korean launderers are creating their own worthless meme coins and pushing stolen Binance funds through them so the coins never move directly from one wallet to another, blockchain analytics firm Elliptic said Monday.

Between Nov. 5 and 7, the operators minted at least six new tokens on the NEAR blockchain — named FUK, OLCCL, MEILI, MMLI, DABI and SINU — paired each with NEAR in its own liquidity pool, then bought the tokens with stolen NEAR and sold them back out through a second set of wallets. About 117,000 NEAR, or roughly $430,000, passed through the six tokens, Elliptic wrote.

The trail sits inside the broader laundering of the Oct. 29 Binance theft, which the exchange has put at $6.34 billion and which the FBI has attributed to North Korea’s TraderTraitor group. Elliptic’s wider update now puts roughly 3,000 BTC — about $225 million — in new bitcoin addresses converted from other stolen assets, while about 11.8 million NEAR, or $43.7 million, from the theft has still not moved.

“Most people think of meme tokens as joke coins whose prices rise and fall on hype,” Elliptic co-founder and chief scientist Dr. Tom Robinson wrote. “North Korea’s launderers have found a different use for them.”

The technique is a four-step hop. The North Korea launderers create a token with a public launch tool, open a liquidity pool pairing it with a real asset such as NEAR or a stablecoin, buy the new token with stolen funds from one set of wallets, then sell it back into the same pool from a second set of wallets that collect the real asset.

At no point do the stolen funds transfer directly from a buying wallet to a collecting wallet, because the pool sits in between. To a casual observer, the collecting wallets look like ordinary traders who sold a token at a profit.

Each of the six NEAR tokens cost about 25 NEAR, or around $90, to create. Each was traded heavily within hours using stolen NEAR and then abandoned. None has traded since Nov. 8, and that afternoon the creators of four of them withdrew what liquidity was left. Five of the six token creators were funded by a wallet that had just sold an earlier token, between two and 13 minutes before the next launch, so the tokens form a chain.

Elliptic first reported the same approach in the laundering of the February 2025 Bybit theft, also attributed to North Korean group TraderTraitor, when a worthless Tron token helped move at least $24 million. In the Binance case the firm has also seen the technique on Tron and a close variant on Solana that used existing thinly traded tokens rather than homemade ones.

The meme coins do not hide the funds. Every step is public on-chain. What they buy is time. Each extra hop slows investigators while launderers race to move funds before they are flagged, and at about $90 apiece the tokens are cheap enough to throw away.

That fits the larger pattern Elliptic has tracked since the theft: more than 20,000 wallets on 16 blockchains, at least 36 services and at least 65,000 transactions. Binance has said the attack began with a fake job interview and ran for five months before any coins moved. Blockchain firms linked the breach to TraderTraitor within a day.

The conversion into bitcoin is the bigger dollar story. All of the stolen SOL has already been swapped, bridged or mixed, and much of the XRP route has fed into bitcoin as well. About $2.85 billion of the original haul has been frozen. A negligible amount has likely been cashed out so far, Elliptic said.

North Korea’s crypto theft machine is not new. Elliptic put the North Korean regime’s 2025 haul above $2 billion, driven by the $1.46 billion Bybit exploit, and its cumulative known total above $6 billion even before Binance.

Binance has reopened withdrawals in stages and said it covered customer losses from its own funds. The test for investigators is whether homemade liquidity pools keep multiplying faster than the blocklists catching up to them.

#5106. Anthropic IPO Valuation Could Be Slashed in Half to $1T After AI Stock Rout: Report

Anthropic IPO Valuation Could Be Slashed in Half to $1T After AI Stock Rout: Report

Major investors are increasingly doubtful that Anthropic's initial public offering can go ahead at its expected valuation of $1.5 trillion, after a sharp sell-off in AI stocks, The Wall Street Journal reported on Thursday.

Anthropic, which filed last month to list on Nasdaq under the ticker "ANT," has been aiming to go public the week before Thanksgiving and raise up to $100 billion, according to the Journal. But three major investors who plan to take part in the IPO told the paper the valuation will likely need to come down, with one suggesting it could be closer to $1 trillion.

The company could also raise less than planned, perhaps around $80 billion, one investor told the Journal. Another wondered whether Anthropic might delay the offering until market conditions improve. Anthropic executives and advisers are expected to meet in the coming days to discuss the plans, according to the report.

The doubts follow a rout in AI stocks on Thursday, as investors weighed whether Democratic gains in the midterms could bring stricter oversight of data center construction. The S&P 500 closed down 4.4% and the tech-heavy Nasdaq 100 fell 6.3%, both their worst days since April 2025, while the Philadelphia semiconductor index slid 12.3% and Nvidia dropped 9.2%, NBC News reported. Stocks partly recovered on Friday morning: as of 11:21 a.m. ET, the Nasdaq Composite was up 1.4% and Nvidia had gained 3.4%, according to Yahoo Finance.

Democrats are planning bills to shield consumers from rising electricity costs tied to data centers, require independent testing of advanced AI systems and clarify when AI companies can be held liable for harm, Reuters reported, though any legislation would likely face steep odds while Trump remains in the White House. The party also plans closer scrutiny of leading AI companies, including Anthropic, according to the report.

Sen. Bernie Sanders (I-VT) has called for a "national data center moratorium," while Texas governor-elect Gina Hinojosa has pledged to pause data center approvals in a state that is home to more than a quarter of planned U.S. data center capacity, CNBC reported.

A delay to Anthropic's IPO is now an "escalating risk" in the current environment, technology analyst Richard Windsor told The Telegraph. "A postponement would protect against near-term valuation risks and would be more likely if the correction sustains over a longer period," said Nalin Patel, an analyst at PitchBook, according to the paper.

Some investors want to see Anthropic's October results before assuming a cut. The company's annualized revenue run rate topped $80 billion at the end of October, and it had $146.6 billion in cash as of September 30, according to the Journal.

Traders have pushed back their expectations. On Polymarket, the odds that Anthropic lists by November 20 stood at 43% on Friday, while the chance of a listing by the end of the year was 71%. On Kalshi, where traders bet on when Anthropic will officially confirm its IPO, the odds of that happening before December 1 fell 15 points to 59%, while the chance of it happening before January 1 slipped to 68%. On Decrypt Predict, a prediction market developed by Decrypt's parent company, Dastan, traders also put the odds of an Anthropic IPO before 2027 at 71%.

The valuation now in question is already well below earlier expectations. In September, Decrypt reported that a leaked draft filing showed Anthropic was seeking a valuation of around $2 trillion.

This article was drafted with the assistance of Claude, an AI model developed by Anthropic.

#5104. US Treasury cuts off MEXC over alleged $16B in illicit transactions

The US Treasury bureau FinCEN on Friday ordered US financial institutions to stop processing transfers involving MEXC Global.

The US Department of Treasury on Friday issued an order to cut cryptocurrency exchange MEXC Global off from the US’ financial system after accusing it of facilitating at least $16 billion in transactions linked to sanctions evasion, cybercrime and other illicit activity.

The Treasury’s Financial Crimes Enforcement Network (FinCEN) asserted that MEXC handled transactions involving sanctioned groups in Iran, Russia and North Korea between May 2021 and May 2026. This included around $1.5 billion in transactions linked to Russian sanctions evasion, according to the official press release.

The order effectively bans US financial institutions from processing money transfers involving MEXC and will take effect 30 days after it is published in the Federal Register.

“Financial institutions cannot have it both ways: Either you support the Iranian regime, or you have access to the U.S. financial system,” said Secretary of the Treasury Scott Bessent. “Treasury will continue to sever the Iranian regime’s economic lifelines and thwart its desperate attempts to circumvent U.S. sanctions using digital assets.”

The action is part of Operation Economic Outcast, a US Treasury campaign launched in August with aims to cut off financial entities that help Iran evade sanctions.

According to DefiLlama, the exchange held about $4.48 billion in total assets as of Friday. Cointelegraph contacted MEXC for comment but had not received a response by publication.

#5103. Formation notifies SEC to operate first tokenized securities venue on Solana

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Launch is expected in early 2027, with tokenized shares of Exodus Movement, Forward Industries, and Galaxy Digital among the initial offerings.

Formation Securities announced today that it has notified the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission of its intention to operate a tokenized securities venue on Solana Solana (SOL).

The firm plans to operate under the SEC's innovation exemption that allows the conditional secondary trading of eligible tokenized stocks through permissioned automated market maker liquidity pools on qualified venues.

Formation wrote in a statement that trading is targeted to start in early 2027, with tokenized shares of Exodus Movement Exodus Movement, Inc. (EXOD), Forward Industries Forward Industries, Inc. (FWDI), and Galaxy Digital Galaxy Digital Holdings (GLXY) set to roll out at launch.

The tokenized shares will be offered to verified investors through Superstate, Securities, Formation Securities' own platform, other distribution channels such as Alpaca, and custodians including Anchorage and BitGo Bank.

Formation stated that it chose Solana for its reliability, speed, low cost, and its support of more than $5 billion in tokenized real-world assets. Its tokenized shares will provide the same voting and dividend rights as other shareholders. Meanwhile, the investors will be able to trade the shares around-the-clock against stablecoins and other eligible assets, settled atomically onchain.

The announcement from Formation came after OKXICE, the joint venture between crypto exchange OKX and NYSE parent Intercontinental Exchange, announced that they would list and trade tokenized shares under the SEC's innovation exemption. While the venue is not live, a total of 67 firms have objected to the tokenization of their shares for trading on the platform.

Amid the growing number of objections that OKXICE is facing, Formation said it plans to work together with issuers on tokenized shares.

"Tokenized stocks will succeed only if public companies see the value in them, and we intend to build this market together with issuers," said Christopher Montagano, president of Formation Securities. "Formation Securities is designed to give issuers and investors a new, transparent market, with trading beyond traditional market hours and faster onchain settlement, while preserving the key rights shareholders enjoy in the traditional financial space."

Formation was established last month as a result of the merger between Solana-based decentralized exchange Orca and Loopscale to focus on tokenization and onchain finance. It said in a press release that it is already working with Figure, Shinhan Asset Management, Superstate, R3, and Securitize.

#5101. Elizabeth Warren Plans Prediction Market Crackdown as She Prepares to Chair Senate Banking

Sen. Elizabeth Warren plans to crack down on prediction markets once she takes the Senate Banking Committee gavel in January, with a banking aide telling Punchbowl News that Warren would seek to stop the sector’s “Wild West-style lack of oversight.”

The Massachusetts Democrat is poised to chair the panel after Democrats swept both chambers of Congress last week, putting her in charge of a portfolio that covers banking, housing, the Federal Reserve and cryptocurrency. Punchbowl published the aide’s rundown of her agenda on Monday.

Warren intends to use the committee’s investigative powers for oversight of prediction markets and election betting platforms, aimed at insider trading and platform manipulation — the same fight she opened before the midterms.

That earlier push was bipartisan. On Oct. 28, Warren and Sen. John Curtis, R-Utah, wrote to Kalshi CEO Tarek Mansour and Polymarket CEO Shayne Coplan asking how the companies stop candidates, campaign staff and others with material nonpublic information from trading election contracts.

“We are particularly troubled by election-related event contracts,” the senators wrote. “Event contracts tied to elections receive prominent coverage and are treated by some observers as indicators of political momentum. There are also documented cases of candidates betting on their own races, which undermines the overall integrity of the American electoral process.”

The letter cited candidates who traded on their own contests on Kalshi, including a February 2026 California gubernatorial race, and pointed to research alleging wash trading and volume manipulation on Polymarket. It also referenced an NPR investigation of campaign insiders who said they traded ahead of polling releases.

“The identification of isolated instances of improper trading does not demonstrate that these markets are adequately protected,” Warren and Curtis wrote. “Instead, it raises serious questions about how much similar activity may be going undetected.” They asked for written answers by Election Day, Nov. 3.

Kalshi’s head of enforcement, Robert DeNault, told Business Insider that the company “proactively blocks candidates and campaign staff from trading to protect market integrity.” Polymarket did not respond to that request for comment.

Prediction markets are not a side show for bitcoin readers. Polymarket is a crypto-native venue that settled U.S. access through a CFTC-registered exchange after years offshore, while Kalshi operates as a designated contract market under the Commodity Futures Trading Commission. Both sit in the orbit of the same committee Warren is about to run.

Under Chairman Michael S. Selig, the CFTC has spent 2026 defending those platforms against state gambling laws, suing Wisconsin, New York and others to assert exclusive federal jurisdiction over event contracts on registered exchanges. A Democratic Banking chair targeting what her aide called a Wild West lands as the counterweight to that stance.

Warren has been one of crypto’s sharpest critics on the Hill. She opposed the CLARITY Act market structure bill, which failed a Senate cloture vote 49-50 on Sept. 15, and analysts have argued a Democratic Congress leaves little path for a Republican rewrite. House Democrats are already sketching their own package with a bigger SEC role.

The aide’s broader agenda for Warren also includes affordability legislation, a push to cap credit card interest rates, AI chip export controls, and oversight of Trump administration officials, Wall Street CEOs, Big Tech executives and anyone tied to the Trump family’s crypto ventures.

The test for prediction markets is no longer whether Trump-era regulators will keep clearing the way. It is whether Warren, with subpoena power and the Banking gavel, turns the Kalshi and Polymarket letter into hearings that rewrite how election and event contracts are policed.

#5100. Crypto Liquidations Top $1 Billion as Bitcoin Tests 11-Week Low

Crypto Liquidations Top $1 Billion as Bitcoin Tests 11-Week Low

Bitcoin is trading near $75,570 on Friday after touching $74,780.91 today; its lowest intraday reading since August 21, 77 days earlier.

The crypto market retreat followed a brutal selloff in AI stocks and another round of forced closures in derivatives markets.

According to CoinGlass data, $1.05 billion in crypto have been liquidated over the preceding 24 hours. Long positions, which profit when prices rise, accounted for $930.04 million, or 88.2%, compared with $124.69 million in shorts.

That 80%-20% gap in liquidations also matches the overall sentiment tracked by the Fear and Greed Index which is set at 28 points (approaching extreme fear) out of 100, where 0 is a state of maximum fear among traders, and 100 is maximum greed. For context, the index was at a neutral just a week ago, before the midterm elections.

Bitcoin accounted for $267.20 million of the total, with 88.9% from long positions, while Ethereum recorded $167.14 million, 81.1% of it long.

Liquidation happens when an exchange automatically closes a position because its collateral is no longer sufficient, sometimes adding selling pressure to an existing decline. Unlike normal trading, in which investors can handle any unrealized loss, trading with leverage gives a maneuver window that can end in the loss of all funds once an asset goes beyond or below a specific price.

The post-midterm election fear is spreading on Wall Street, and U.S. technology stocks took the first big hit on Thursday. The Nasdaq Composite fell 5.52%, its worst session in 19 months, while the Philadelphia semiconductor index plunged more than 12% and Nvidia alone lost over 9%.

Democratic victories in Tuesday's midterm elections raised concerns that tougher AI regulation could slow construction of the data centers behind this year's chip rally. The S&P 500 lost 4.36% on Thursday, erasing roughly $3 trillion in market value across its constituent companies.

Bitcoin had already given back its post-election relief rally as chipmakers sank, as Decrypt reported Thursday. Forced closures of bullish crypto positions accelerated the fall, though simultaneous moves in stocks and tokens doesn’t necessarily mean the equity rout alone caused Bitcoin's decline.

A stronger-than-expected U.S. October jobs report also increased expectations of another Federal Reserve rate hike in December. Higher borrowing costs can weigh on speculative markets, leaving crypto exposed even as chip shares recover.

Right now, Bitcoin trades near $75,570, with the current candle still forming after a low of $74,780.91. The weekly chart shows another red candle and a sequence of lower highs since September, when Bitcoin briefly traded above $87,000.

The 50-day exponential moving average, or EMA, gives more weight to recent prices and sits near $79,756, above the 200-day EMA around $76,497. That ordering remains bullish on a longer view, but Bitcoin is now trading below both averages, a sign the latest decline has overtaken the lagging bullish signal.

The Relative Strength Index, or RSI, reads 37.7, below the neutral 50 mark but still above the 30 level generally considered oversold. The Average Directional Index, or ADX, is at 19.7, typical of trendless states—but the negative directional indicator exceeds the positive one, pointing to weak but bearish directional pressure.

The immediate support is $74,780, the horizontal line and intraday low on the supplied chart. A daily close below it would shift attention toward the $73,000 to $72,500 area around August's breakout, followed by the psychological $70,000 level if that zone fails.

On a recovery, Bitcoin first faces the 200-day EMA near $76,497, then some weak and immediate near $80,000. Friday's daily candle is still open, with its $74,780.91 low the immediate level to defend.

#5099. Bitcoin loses 50-week moving average as 24-hour crypto long liquidations top $1 billion

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Bitcoin ended a seven-week streak above the key technical level, while around 170,000 traders were liquidated over 24 hours.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) closed below its 50-week moving average on Sunday for the first time in eight weeks, before briefly falling below $74,000 into Monday morning as more than $1 billion in crypto long positions were liquidated in 24 hours.

Total liquidations exceeded $1.15 billion, including $1.04 billion in longs and $117 million in shorts, according to CoinGlass data. Ether traders accounted for over $500 million in liquidations, compared with around $309 million for bitcoin, with CoinGlass recording a total of 167,764 traders liquidated during the period. The largest individual order was a $48.2 million ETH-USDT long on Binance, according to the platform. Liquidation data is imperfect, with Binance and OKX still underreporting in incomplete bursts, for example, so total amounts are likely to be higher.

U.S. spot bitcoin ETFs also ended the week with $1.28 billion in net outflows, the most since late June, The Block reported on Saturday. The 12 funds lost money on four of the five trading days last week and flipped negative for the year, with a combined total of $914.1 million in net outflows for 2026. BlackRock's IBIT alone shed $403 million on Friday.

Alex Thorn, head of firmwide research at Galaxy, noted that bitcoin closed the week at around 0.7% below its 50-week moving average of $75,488. Bitcoin previously reclaimed the level on Sept. 20 and held above it for seven consecutive weeks before Sunday's close.

Thorn looked back at the 2011-2012 and 2019-2020 bear market recoveries, when bitcoin also lost the 50-week average after initially getting back above it. In the first case, bitcoin spent another 20 weeks below the line and fell as much as 35% beneath it. In 2019-2020, bitcoin fell back below the average five times, including an eight-week stretch during the Covid crash. Nevertheless, in both cases, the earlier bear market low held on a weekly closing basis, he said.

However, Thorn noted that his sample covered just five reclaims and that the bottom is only known in hindsight. Bitcoin's previous low for the current cycle was $59,517 on June 28, with the firm arguing last month that the bear market was over.

"We believe the bottom is in, selling pressure has diminished, the debasement case has strengthened, and the crowd has left," Thorn wrote at the time. "Our conclusion is simple: we believe now is an attractive time to buy and hold bitcoin."

Thorn said on Monday that his bullish technical case from October would remain valid if bitcoin regained the 50-week average within a month.

#5097. ‘Connecting the dots’: Michael Saylor’s Strategy buys another 1,036 bitcoin for $82 million as holdings top 850,000 BTC

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Strategy has scooped up another 1,036 BTC for approximately $81.9 million at an average price of $79,012 per bitcoin.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) treasury company Strategy Strategy, Inc. (MSTR) bought another 1,036 BTC for $81.9 million at an average price of $79,012 per bitcoin between Nov. 2 and Nov. 8, according to a Monday filing with the Securities and Exchange Commission.

Strategy's bitcoin holdings now total 850,364 BTC, representing more than 4% of bitcoin's total supply cap. The firm's total holdings were acquired for a cost of approximately $64.2 billion at an average price of $75,456 per bitcoin, including fees and expenses.

Strategy used proceeds from at-the-market sales of its Class A common stock, MSTR, for the latest bitcoin acquisitions. Last week, Strategy sold 2,051,348 MSTR shares for approximately $283.8 million. Approximately $18.34 billion worth of MSTR shares remain available for issuance and sale under that ATM program, the firm said in the filing.

Additionally, the company also used its USD Reserve to repurchase 1,887,464 STRC preferred shares between Nov. 2 and Nov. 8 for roughly $188.3 million, and $2.1 million from the reserve to fund dividends on its preferred stocks.

As of Nov. 8, Strategy's USD Reserve and USD Cash balances were $4.77 billion and $146.2 million, respectively, according to the filing.

Strategy co-founder and Executive Chairman Michael Saylor posted a Strategy bitcoin acquisition tracker chart on Sunday, alongside the statement, "Connecting the dots," indicating another potential acquisition announcement ahead of Monday's filing.

Strategy remains by far the largest of nearly 200 public companies that have launched a bitcoin treasury model. Metaplanet, Tether-backed Twenty One, Strive, and MARA make up the remainder of the top five, with 44,000 BTC, 43,514 BTC, 43,124 BTC, and 35,577 BTC, respectively, according to data from Bitcoin Treasuries.

MSTR fell 2.1% last week, closing at $128.93 on Friday, according to The Block's Strategy price page, while bitcoin slipped 3.2% during the same period.

#5094. Nvidia may face a more hostile post-election Washington: Report

CEO Jensen Huang’s relationship with President Donald Trump probably won’t save the company from a more adversarial Washington, Semafor says.

Chipmaker Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang’s relationship with President Donald Trump probably won’t save the company from a more hostile Washington in the post-election era, according to Semafor.

Even as victorious Democrats look to take the lead in regulatory committees, Republicans are planning to impose tougher export controls on chips.

Three Republican House members have asked Huang to appear in a briefing on whether he had been targeted by a Chinese intelligence agent, according to Semafor’s report on Friday.

#5093. NYSE to settle tokenized stocks on Avalanche in planned 24/7 market

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

NYSE says the venue will support equities issued on multiple blockchains, while Avalanche will underpin the ledger used to record and settle trades.

The New York Stock Exchange has selected Avalanche blockchain for clearing and settlement on its planned 24/7 tokenized-securities platform, Ava Labs said in a statement on Monday.

The platform is being designed to support around-the-clock trading, clearing, and settlement of tokenized securities, pending regulatory approval. It is targeting a fourth-quarter launch and will use NYSE’s existing technology to match trades, according to The Wall Street Journal.

"It will support the platform's 24/7 atomic settlement needs with dedicated infrastructure with predictable performance and costs, the control to meet its regulatory and operational requirements, and connectivity to a broader ecosystem of assets and liquidity," Ava Labs CEO Emin Gün Sirer said in the statement.

The selection follows about a year of testing. Ava Labs President Charley Cooper told The Block in September that NYSE had spent the previous year testing Avalanche and working with the company on how the technology could fit into its systems.

Cooper said NYSE had also examined the project’s economics and technology, but stopped short of confirming a selection. ICE Head of Strategic Initiatives Michael Blaugrund said at the time that the exchange operator was "very engaged" with Avalanche.

NYSE Head of Digital Issuance Dennis Lee said the exchange is evaluating different technologies for different parts of the platform. The venue will support equities listed on a range of blockchains, while its settlement ledger needs to provide speed, reliability, predictable costs, and control over who can participate, he said.

The exchange has also enlisted more partners supporting the venue. Last week, it announced agreements with Broadridge Financial Solutions, Computershare, Equiniti, and Superstate to support issuer-sponsored tokenized securities and transfer-agent functions. Securitize and tZERO were already listed as partners.

#5092. Amundi Boosts Strategy (MSTR) Stake 44% to $291 Million as Shares Slide

Amundi, Europe’s largest asset manager, raised its stake in Strategy (MSTR) by about 44% in the third quarter, a Form 13F filed Nov. 6 shows, lifting the position to 1,903,725 shares worth $291.4 million at Sept. 30 prices.

That is up 579,672 shares from the 1,324,053 Amundi reported at June 30, when the same holding was valued at about $115.1 million. The Paris-based firm managed €2.579 trillion in assets at the end of September, up 11% year over year, and described itself as Europe’s leading asset manager.

The filing lands as MSTR has given back much of its third-quarter run. Strategy shares closed at $153.09 on Sept. 30 and near $126 on Monday, an 18% drop that would put Amundi’s disclosed stake near $239 million at the latest close, Yahoo Finance data show.

A 13F is a snapshot, not a trading diary. Amundi filed a combination report that folds in 17 other managers across its group, so the Strategy line can include client, fund and discretionary positions rather than a single proprietary bet, and the firm may have changed the holding since Sept. 30.

Strategy itself kept stacking. The company bought another 1,036 bitcoin last week for $81.9 million at an average of $79,012, a Monday 8-K filing and company release show, lifting its treasury to 850,364 BTC acquired for $64.16 billion at an average of $75,456.

That average cost now sits above the spot bitcoin price, leaving the stack underwater on paper even after the firm added 811 bitcoin the prior week. Strategy’s public ledger still ranks it as the largest corporate bitcoin holder, with the stack equal to roughly 4% of bitcoin’s 21 million supply cap.

MSTR roughly doubled during the third quarter itself. The June 30 13F valued Amundi’s then-smaller stake at an implied $86.93 a share, against the $153.09 Sept. 30 close, so the dollar value of the position rose faster than the share count. Since then the stock has tracked bitcoin’s slide into the mid-$70,000s, wiping out a large share of that mark-to-market gain for anyone still holding the Sept. 30 position.

The timing matters because Strategy funds most of its bitcoin buys by selling new MSTR common under an at-the-market program. A weaker share price raises more dilution for every coin purchased, which is the feedback loop that has defined the stock’s moves this year.

Amundi was not the only large European name adding bitcoin exposure on the quarter. Barclays’ own 13F, also filed Nov. 6, shows 3,942,900 shares of BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT) worth $186.7 million at Sept. 30, up 48% from 2,658,147 shares three months earlier, SEC records show.

That Barclays line carries the same caveat: a 13F gathers client, managed and trading book positions, not proof the bank put its own capital into bitcoin. Still, the two filings together sketch the same picture of Q3: traditional European managers kept adding exposure to bitcoin proxies even as the spot market cooled into the fall.

The institutional adds sit against a weaker tape since quarter-end. Bitcoin has slipped into the mid-$70,000s, Strategy stock has followed it down, and the value of Amundi’s disclosed MSTR stake has compressed even as the share count on the filing stayed fixed. At Monday’s close the disclosed stake was about $52 million below its Sept. 30 mark.

Whether Amundi kept buying, trimmed, or simply held through October’s slide will not show up until the next 13F. For now the Nov. 6 filing is the record Europe’s biggest asset manager left: nearly 1.9 million Strategy shares, up 44%, at a $291 million quarter-end mark.

#5090. Suspicious prediction market activity seen before election poll release: NYT

Bets were placed shortly before two recent New York Times/Siena political polls were released to the public, according to a report.

Suspicious prediction market bets were placed shortly before two recent New York Times/Siena political polls were released to the public, according to a report.

Vanderbilt University researchers said a Polymarket user bet almost $200,000 on Oct. 31 that Nebraska Senator Pete Ricketts would be re-elected. An hour later, a Times/Siena poll was released showing Ricketts in the lead.

A Friday New York Times report said while it was not clear if the user had access to inside information, they won more than $77,000.

The user also placed similar large bets on other markets, including Kalshi, the report said.

“There’s a series of circumstances here that it’s hard to write it off as a random occurrence,” said Brett Goldstein of the Vanderbilt Institute of National Security. It “requires further investigation.”

Polymarket said it had spotted the trades and was investigating. Kalshi declined comment.

Cointelegraph reported in April that a US Army soldier had been charged after making more than $400,000 by betting on a prediction market allegedly using nonpublic information tied to a US operation involving then Venezuelan President Nicolás Maduro.

In a statement, the Department of Justice alleged that Master Sergeant Gannon Ken Van Dyke was involved in the planning and execution of Operation Absolute Resolve, which led to the capture of Maduro at a residence in Caracas, Venezuela, in January.

Van Dyke allegedly used this information to buy “yes” shares in related Polymarket contracts, including “Maduro out by January 31” and “Trump invokes War Powers against Venezuela by January 31,” according to the DOJ.

#5089. Crypto Card Provider Gnosis Pay Says Hackers Accessed Customer Data

Crypto Card Provider Gnosis Pay Says Hackers Accessed Customer Data

Crypto card provider Gnosis Pay said Friday that hackers accessed customer information through the system behind its own branded cards, the latest in a string of security incidents across the industry.

The company’s monitoring systems detected unauthorized access on Nov. 3, and containment began within two hours, Gnosis Pay said in a blog post. It has since replaced the compromised security credentials and fixed the vulnerability. Customer funds weren’t affected, the company said.

The breach was limited to Gnosis Pay’s consumer business, whose platform was already scheduled for retirement on Dec. 20. Its enterprise platform, which powers white-label card programs for fintech firms, wallets and neobanks, wasn’t affected.

Gnosis Pay didn’t say how many customers were affected or what data was exposed, but said it emailed each affected customer with details of what was taken from their account.

“While funds were not affected, the security of personal information is just as important and we take full responsibility,” the company wrote on X.

Gnosis Pay said it has stepped up monitoring of card activity and wallets for fraud, and has made 3D Secure, an extra verification step, mandatory for all online card payments until the consumer platform is retired. It has reported the breach to its partners, its card issuer and data protection authorities, and is arranging specialist monitoring for signs that the data is misused.

We had a data breach affecting the system powering Gnosis Pay-branded cards (our B2C business line) that resulted in the exposure of certain customer information.

Everyone affected was sent an email today with full information.

We deeply regret that this happened. While funds…

— Gnosis Pay 💳 (@gnosispay) November 6, 2026

The company urged customers to be wary of unexpected messages about their cards, even ones that include real personal details or purchases. It said it will never ask for seed phrases, PINs or full card numbers, or ask users to move funds.

It also told customers to be careful about sharing their address or details of their crypto holdings, both online and offline.

That last warning reflects a growing threat. Physical “wrench attacks” on crypto holders, including home invasions and kidnappings, were on pace for a record year after more than $30 million was stolen in the first half of 2026, according to blockchain analytics firm Chainalysis.

The disclosure follows several other recent examples of breaches in crypto security. Beyond the record-setting $6.4 billion hack of the world’s largest crypto exchange in Binance, rival trading platform Coinbase said last week that a breach at analytics vendor Amplitude exposed some customer contact data.

Last month, hardware wallet maker Ledger said all confirmed cases in a suspected $87 million drain involved devices bought from CryptoBilis, a Southeast Asian reseller, and urged recent buyers to move their funds to new devices.

#5085. US Sanctions 45 More Targets Tied to Alleged Crypto Scam Empire Prince Group

US Sanctions 45 More Targets Tied to Alleged Crypto Scam Empire Prince Group

The U.S. Treasury Department has sanctioned four individuals and 41 entities that it says support Prince Group, the Cambodian conglomerate accused of running scam compounds that use trafficked workers to defraud victims through crypto investment and romance scams.

The new designations, announced on Thursday by the Office of Foreign Assets Control (OFAC), target the offshore companies, nominee owners and administrators that Treasury says have helped Prince Group executives conceal assets and evade earlier sanctions imposed in October 2025 and June 2026.

"Cyber scam syndicates continue to steal billions of dollars from American victims while relying on sophisticated international business networks to launder their illicit proceeds," Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent said.

Among those sanctioned is Qiu Yong, who Treasury said controls a network of British Virgin Islands and Hong Kong companies on behalf of Prince Group, including money lenders and wine businesses, and who has worked for a company controlled by Prince Group chairman Chen Zhi. His personal assistant, Zhao Mengyao, was also designated.

OFAC also targeted two people it said are holding property for, or running companies with, Prince Group executive Chen Bo, who was sanctioned in June. They include his wife, He Yujing, to whom Chen Bo transferred ownership of his Tokyo mansion after the October 2025 sanctions, according to Treasury, and associate Visal Pang, who controls 11 Cambodian companies spanning construction, logistics, a hotel and a "super app." Eleven companies still controlled by Chen Bo were also sanctioned, including Prince International Airlines and CB General Insurance.

Further entities in Singapore, Mauritius and Malaysia were sanctioned for links to Prince Group associate Dai An, along with a Singapore media company owned by alleged Prince Group finance chief Thet Li.

Separately, OFAC designated Chui Kok Wa, a Malaysian national based in Madagascar, who Treasury said led a network of scammers that used crypto and other technologies to defraud Americans of more than $400,000.

The U.S. is seeking the forfeiture of about $14 billion in Bitcoin tied to Prince Group, while UK authorities have frozen the London penthouse of Thet Li, Decrypt reported last month. Prince Group has denied the allegations.

Chen Zhi himself was arrested in Cambodia in January along with two other Chinese nationals and extradited to China, where state media described him as the head of a major cross-border gambling and fraud syndicate, the BBC reported. Cambodia, which had revoked his citizenship the month before, also placed the group's Prince Bank into liquidation.

Thursday's action was coordinated with the FBI, the Justice Department's Scam Center Strike Force, which has reported $580 million in crypto seizures and freezes, and the U.S. Attorney's Office for the District of Alaska, with help from authorities in the British Virgin Islands and the UK, according to Treasury.

Interpol declared scam compound networks a global threat last year, citing their use of human trafficking, online fraud and coerced labor. In September, the Treasury's Financial Crimes Enforcement Network linked about $12.7 billion in reported financial activity to suspected crypto investment scams, largely run by criminal organizations operating scam compounds in Southeast Asia.

The UN Office on Drugs and Crime estimated in July that scam operations across East and Southeast Asia, Australia and New Zealand cost victims between $88.3 billion and $114.1 billion in 2025, much of it through crypto investment fraud run from compounds holding people from at least 80 countries.

#5083. Prediction market broker River Markets registers affiliate as CFTC futures commission merchant

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

River Markets was founded in May and has routed over 250 million contracts. It's backed by "top-tier venture capital firms" following a $8.5 million seed funding round.

River Markets said its affiliate, River Finance LLC, has registered with the Commodity Futures Trading Commission as a futures commission merchant (FCM) and joined the National Futures Association (NFA), allowing it to carry customer accounts and handle client funds.

The company is building a prime brokerage platform for prediction markets that aims to let institutional traders manage capital, positions, and risk across multiple exchanges and clearinghouses through a single account, with plans to support financing arrangements and permitted over-the-counter trading.

"Our conversations with the institutions entering this market give us a unique view of what they need," Cofounder and CEO Oscar Levy said in a release. "Access to event contracts isn’t enough. They need to use capital efficiently and manage risk around the clock, across exchanges. That’s the brokerage model we’re building."

River Markets was founded in May and serves hedge funds, sports funds, proprietary trading firms, and event-risk hedging companies, according to the firm. River Markets has routed over 250 million contracts and is backed by "top-tier venture capital firms" following its $8.5 million seed funding round.

Combined trading volume across Kalshi, Polymarket US, and Polymarket's international platform exceeded $500 billion in October. Prediction markets are becoming large enough to create demand for the kinds of brokerage, clearing, financing, and risk-management services traditionally associated with derivatives markets.

In July, Polymarket-affiliated entities sought an FCM and swap-firm registration through the NFA to enable U.S. customers to trade using borrowed capital. Kalshi affiliate Kinetic Markets received NFA approval as an FCM and swap firm in March. And last month, Blockchain.com applied for both a designated contract market (DCM) license and FCM registration.

Of note, Hyperliquid Strategies last week filed to become an FCM to carry U.S. client accounts for HIP-3 perpetual futures markets, an effort to help bring permissioned Hyperliquid markets to U.S. customers within a regulated framework.

#5081. US sanctions Canadian money-exchange firms over Iran ties

US also offers as much as $20 million and possible relocation for information about Iran’s nuclear program.

The US Treasury imposed financial sanctions on eight Canadian money-service companies it claims have done business with sanctioned ‌Iranian entities.

These companies facilitated the exchange of Iranian rials and other currencies for digital assets, enabling international transactions, a US Treasury Department statement said on Friday.

The sanctions, which serve to isolate the companies from the dollar-based financial system, are the latest in a series of Treasury moves against firms outside the US.

These businesses have facilitated transactions with sanctioned Iranian digital asset exchanges, as well as with digital asset addresses associated with Iran’s Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps (IRGC), according to the Treasury.

Related: US plans to seize $1B in crypto linked to Iran this week: Scott Bessent

“Canada cannot be a safe haven for illicit financial facilitators that provide sanctions evasion services to the Iranian regime and its proxies,” said Secretary of the Treasury Scott Bessent. “Treasury will pursue and disrupt the Iranian regime’s financial channels anywhere in the world.”

Cointelegraph reported in August that the Treasury Department’s Office of Foreign Assets Control, or OFAC, was targeting crypto exchanges facilitating the transfer of funds to Iran’s IRGC. Bessent said at the time that the US planned to “increase the economic pressure” on Iran, focusing on financial networks funding the country’s regime “in dollars, rials, or crypto.”

Friday’s designations are a continuation of Treasury’s campaign to “disrupt the regime’s exploitation of digital assets to fund terrorism, human rights abuses, and attacks on US personnel and allies,” the statement said.

It added that the actions were coordinated with FINTRAC, Canada’s financial intelligence unit.

Some of the entities subject to this action have changed their names and addresses to evade detection, the statement noted.

In a separate action on Friday, the US Rewards for Justice program offered as much as $20 million and possible relocation for information about Iran’s nuclear program, including its facilities, equipment and personnel.

Iran’s lack of compliance with the International Atomic Energy Agency on safeguards has led to the agency’s loss of “continuity of knowledge” regarding Iran’s production of enriched uranium and components like centrifuges, the statement said.

#5077. Bitcoin holders have received eCash — What can you do with it?

Bitcoin’s eCash fork gives eligible holders an equivalent balance of ECX, but questions remain over where they can trade the tokens and what they can do with them.

Somewhere between the Binance hack and the US midterms, Paul Sztorc’s long-awaited Bitcoin fork, known as the eCash network, went live.

“We need to actively sabotage the ‘degrowth’ policies killing Bitcoin,” Sztorc tells Magazine.

The hard fork gives eligible Bitcoin holders an interesting windfall: their equivalent BTC balance in the new chain’s native token, ECX.

So what benefits does eCash bring to developers, and what can Bitcoin HODLers actually do with their new coins?

Developed by Sztorc’s LayerTwo Labs, eCash is a separate hard fork of Bitcoin born out of Sztorc’s frustration with some of the “complacency narratives” and “cult-like toxicity” he believes has been holding Bitcoin back.

Sztorc dismisses criticism of Drivechains’ security. Source: archive.ecash

Its main innovation is Drivechains, a system that gives developers freedom to build independent sidechains with different features without having to make changes to Bitcoin’s base-layer protocol (or in this case, eCash’s base layer).

Five sidechains launched alongside eCash’s mainnet: BitNames, BitAssets, Thunder, Truthcoin and zSide, with two more, the quantum-resistant Photon and decentralized exchange CoinShift, still to come.

Related: Blockchain developer Paul Sztorc announces BTC hard fork eCash

Sztorc has spent over a decade advocating for Drivechains and believes that Bitcoin’s notoriously conservative development approach has held back its potential.

“Anything that isn’t maximizing network usage is simply a distraction,” he says. “Users, the ones who actually pay transaction fees to miners, will win in the long run.”

The eCash fork took place at Bitcoin block 973,728, and anyone who controlled their private keys at the snapshot received an equivalent ECX balance.

“If you held the keys to your BTC at that exact block, you now own the exact same amount of ECX,” LayerTwo Labs tells Magazine. “It is that simple, and you can access them right now.”

Like beauty, simplicity is in the eye of the beholder, and Bitcoiners who self-custody their coins will need to use a wallet that supports eCash and carefully follow its process to separate the ECX from their BTC.

Because the two networks share transaction history, an unprotected transaction on one chain could be replayed on the other.

“Never move your BTC first, as an ordinary, unprotected BTC spend can easily be replayed on the ECX network,” LayerTwo Labs warns. Replay controls are built into the protocol, it says, but the company advises holders to move ECX first, before spending BTC.

Anyone keeping BTC on a centralized exchange is “at their mercy regarding distribution,” and should check whether their exchange supports the fork or has announced an ECX distribution.

Accessing ECX isn’t the same thing as being able to sell it and the tokens are not yet widely available on larger crypto exchanges. Depending on which country you are in, there can be tax implications from Bitcoin forks, which are worth looking up before filing your next tax return.

NeoxEX said it opened native ECX deposits, withdrawals and trading on Oct. 29, with ECX/USDT, ECX/USDC and ECX/BTC pairs, while CoinW announced plans to distribute ECX to eligible Bitcoin holders, with deposits, withdrawals and trading to be announced at a later date.

Orca, a decentralized exchange on Solana, lets users swap USDC for wrapped ECX (wECX) through a liquidity pool. That gives holders another way to trade exposure to ECX, but it involves a wrapped token on a different blockchain rather than native ECX trading on the eCash network.

Related: Binance, Polymarket seek Polygon hard fork to recover $6.8M in stolen crypto

How much are the tokens worth? CoinMarketCap placed ECX at $32.63, with around $355,000 in 24-hour trading volume as of Nov. 5, while CoinGecko shows the token as a preview listing, with no price yet available and no tracked exchange markets. Crypto market data provider Kaiko says it does not track ECX, which limits the availability of independent market data.

LayerTwo Labs says early trading saw ECX fluctuate between $40 and $50, but it did not provide supporting order book depth or trading volume figures, pointing instead to the Orca pool’s total value locked as a way for users to assess available liquidity.

eCash is $32.63 on CoinMarketCap as of Nov. 5

“If our fork replicates even a fraction of [Bitcoin Cash] BCH’s initial performance,” Sztorc says, “the liquidity will be staggering,” adding that anyone concerned about low liquidity can hold their coins and decide later.

“We are actively adding new exchanges and trading pairs, so the user experience will only improve,” he says. “Otherwise, you’re welcome to take your free money and leave.”

Sztorc says the value of eCash will ultimately depend on whether developers build useful applications and users are willing to pay for them. Paying customers and transaction fee revenue are the project’s main measures of success during its first year, he says.

He’s not concerned about criticism of Drivechains’ security model, especially the “superstition” that miners could coordinate to steal funds held on sidechains.

“If you genuinely believe miners will torch their own billion-dollar operations for a short-term theft on an L2, then you shouldn’t trust Bitcoin’s L1 either,” he says.

So for now, eCash gives Bitcoiners a new asset and developers a new playground — but whether ECX becomes much more than that will depend on whether anyone actually wants to use it.

#5076. Bitcoin ETFs Bleed $430 Million as Tech Rout Tests Wall Street Buyers

Bitcoin ETFs Bleed $430 Million as Tech Rout Tests Wall Street Buyers

U.S. spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds lost $430.2 million to net withdrawals Thursday as a technology-stock selloff spread to crypto, with Fidelity and BlackRock leading the exits.

Fidelity's Wise Origin Bitcoin Fund (FBTC) posted $199.9 million in net outflows and BlackRock's iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT) shed $105 million, according to Farside Investors. Together they accounted for about 71% of the day's withdrawals.

Grayscale's GBTC lost another $49.9 million, while Franklin Templeton's EZBC shed $32.9 million. No fund in Farside's table recorded positive net flows Thursday, although several reported no net movement.

The latest withdrawals brought this week's net outflow to $869.8 million, following a $643.2 million outflow last Friday. Wednesday's modest $74.6 million inflow offered a brief reprieve before Thursday's reversal.

Fidelity's swing was particularly sharp: FBTC attracted $236.7 million Wednesday, then surrendered nearly $200 million the next session. BlackRock, meanwhile, lost $201.2 million even on Wednesday's positive day, extending a five-session withdrawal streak since October 30 to roughly $1.04 billion.

ETF flows have often been treated as a gauge of demand from investors accessing Bitcoin through regular brokerage accounts. The latest figures show that access works both ways: the same familiar wrapper that makes buying easy also makes exiting straightforward.

Because ETFs trade on stock exchanges, investors can adjust their Bitcoin exposure without transferring coins to a crypto wallet. That convenience can also make the funds an easy place to cut risk during an equity selloff. Daily net flows capture shares created or redeemed at the fund level, not every trade between investors.

Thursday's redemptions arrived as the Nasdaq Composite plunged 5.5% and the S&P 500 fell about 4.4%. Chipmakers and data-center stocks led the decline as investors questioned the pace of artificial-intelligence spending and potential restrictions after the U.S. midterm elections.

Bitcoin also surrendered its post-election rebound, falling back toward $76,000 after trading above $80,000 Wednesday. Short liquidations had helped power that rally, making the subsequent reversal more painful for leveraged traders.

The ETF figures add another piece to that picture. Net flows measure creations minus redemptions of ETF shares; they cannot tell whether holders were raising cash to cover losses elsewhere, trimming Bitcoin exposure, or rebalancing portfolios.

#5074. Binance hack launderers mint homemade Elon Musk, animal memecoins to move stolen funds: Elliptic

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Six throwaway tokens helped obfuscate stolen NEAR as trading proceeds, repeating a tactic used after the 2025 Bybit hack.

North Korea's money launderers are spinning up throwaway memecoins to help obfuscate funds stolen in the $6.4 billion Binance hack, according to blockchain analytics firm Elliptic.

In a new report released Monday, Elliptic said about 117,000 NEAR, worth roughly $430,000 at the time, were routed into six newly created memecoins created between Nov. 5 and Nov. 7.

Like many other memecoins, the tokens feature animal mascots and one sporting an expletive ticker. Another features Elon Musk under an "Elon 2028 President" banner. Elliptic added that there was no suggestion that Musk himself was linked to it.

The launderers can then use decentralized exchange liquidity pools to separate wallets holding stolen funds from wallets taking in proceeds. One set of wallets will buy the memecoins with stolen NEAR, while another sells the token into the pool and collects NEAR in return.

"At no point do the stolen funds move directly from a buying wallet to a collecting wallet, because the pool sits in between," Elliptic wrote. "To a casual observer, the collecting wallets look like ordinary traders who have sold a token at a profit."

The tokens have all since been abandoned, with Elliptic adding that much of the NEAR collected went into stablecoins or cross-chain bridges.

Elliptic noted that this same tactic was used following the 2025 Bybit hack, with launderers moving around $24 million through a homemade token paired with a stablecoin on Tron.

In the Binance case, the firm also found evidence of a similar approach on Solana using existing tokens that had low liquidity.

The transfers are still public and traceable on the blockchain. Elliptic said the additional swaps are only meant to be hurdles to slow investigators down, giving the launderers a little extra time to move funds before their wallets get flagged.

The amounts moved through the six NEAR tokens are still small compared to the 11.8 million NEAR worth around $42.7 million at current prices.

This follows Elliptic's update from Friday, which tracked roughly $124 million in stolen assets converted into bitcoin. Binance's earlier incident report said that the attack had been in the works for months and involved malicious developer software, a fake job interview, and compromised AI tooling.

The exchange said the FBI linked the attack to North Korea.

#5070. Coinbase and PNC Bank expand partnership with USDC payments for corporate clients

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The pilot program will automatically convert incoming stablecoin payments into U.S. dollars, following the launch of bitcoin trading for PNC's private banking customers last year.

PNC Bank is expanding its partnership with crypto exchange Coinbase Coinbase Global, Inc. (COIN) to let corporate and institutional clients receive payments in USDC, according to an announcement on Monday. The service is initially being offered through a pilot program for eligible customers.

The bank will automatically convert incoming USDC payments into U.S. dollars at a 1-to-1 rate before depositing the funds into clients' designated PNC accounts. The pilot uses Coinbase Payment APIs for stablecoin transactions, alongside PNC's banking and treasury management services. PNC said it would complement the faster-payment options already available to its corporate clients.

PNC first announced its partnership with Coinbase in July 2025, agreeing to use the exchange's Crypto-as-a-Service platform while providing select banking services to Coinbase. In December, the bank began offering spot bitcoin trading to eligible private banking clients, allowing them to buy and sell bitcoin directly through their existing investment accounts.

Coinbase has also been working with other banks on stablecoin payments. In September, it expanded its partnership with Citi to connect traditional fiat payments with stablecoins. The arrangement allows Coinbase customers to use Citi's Virtual Account Wallet to receive and manage fiat payments, with automatic conversion into stablecoins. Citi's institutional clients can also accept stablecoin payments through its Spring platform, using Coinbase's infrastructure to convert them into fiat for settlement.

Earlier this year, Coinbase also partnered with payments firm Moov to bring stablecoin acceptance, settlement, and real-time funding capabilities to systems used by more than 1,000 community banks and credit unions using Coinbase's Payments API and custodial wallets.

#5068. Paraguay Protests Over Bank Lockout Intensify as Impeachment Vote Nears

Paraguay Protests Over Bank Lockout Intensify as Impeachment Vote Nears

Thousands of Paraguayans marched through Asunción this week demanding their money back after Ueno Bank, a digital lender, locked about 3 million customers out of their accounts, according.

That is about 60% of Paraguay’s adults. The bank also held $800 million to $850 million in public money, including state pension and health funds, according to the AP, so the fallout reaches the pension system.

Anger boiled over Thursday night outside Ueno’s headquarters in Asunción, where savers burned debit and credit cards. Paraguayo “Payo” Cubas, a former presidential candidate, tried to join and was booed, shoved, and forced out.

Protesters shouted “divisor” and “oportunista,” meaning divider and opportunist, accusing him of trying to profit politically from public rage, according to the same report.

Diario Hoy reported that protesters also blamed him for the Senate’s current makeup, saying Cubas put Zenaida Delgado and Javier Vera on his party’s legislative ticket, the slate of candidates voters choose from, and that both joined the government’s side soon after taking office.

#Nacionales | 😱 Echan a "Payo" Cubas de la manifestación

🔹 Paraguayo “Payo” Cubas fue rechazado por los manifestantes cuando intentó acercarse a la protesta. Los presentes lo increparon y le gritaron “divisor” y “oportunista”.

➡️ Además, cuestionaron su responsabilidad en el… pic.twitter.com/odHzVvGPO3

— Universo 970 AM (@Universo970py) November 6, 2026

President Santiago Peña, a shareholder in Ueno’s holding company until March 2025, submitted his resignation Thursday, but a joint session of Congress called to accept it adjourned without a quorum, according to La Nación. He then said he would face impeachment—a trial in Congress to remove a president—and defend himself.

The lower house had already voted 78-0 to open impeachment, and on Friday the Senate rejected his request for 72 extra hours to prepare his defense. The Senate planned a removal vote for Friday night, ABC Color reported, with Vice President Pedro Alliana next in line.

Horacio Cartes, the Colorado Party leader and Peña’s former political mentor, withdrew his support and backed the impeachment. “We will not be on the side of the plunder of public, private and retirees’ funds,” Cartes said.

Interior Minister Enrique Riera and central bank president Carlos Carvallo have resigned, and eight Ueno executives have been arrested, according to La Nación’s Friday update. Prosecutors also opened a criminal investigation into Peña’s possible responsibility for the bank’s irregularities, which he denies.

Police used water cannons and tear gas on marchers near Congress, and a new law requires most deposits to be returned within five business days, though protesters have not been placated.

The central bank says its deposit guarantee covers up to 228.3 million guaraníes per person, about $36,000, but as Decrypt previously reported, the first payouts will go only to accounts with up to 10 million guaraníes (around $1,600) and no debts to Ueno.

Bitcoiners, of course, rushed to use Paraguay’s banking crisis as an example on the benefits of decentralization and self custody, and why the “Bitcoin fixes this” motto is still relevant in today’s financial system. “Bitcoin adoption at a nation-state level can mitigate these types of situations,” said JAN3 founder Samson Mow. “If BTC is available to the people as a parallel rail, then systemic banking failures have far less impact.”

#5067. Ex-FBI counterintelligence agent stole almost $2.5M in crypto

Yaroch paid off his debts and intended to use the proceeds to finance a new life in Portugal, where he planned to buy a vineyard.

A former FBI counterintelligence agent admitted to wire fraud and money laundering in stealing almost $2.5 million in cryptocurrency from unidentified adversarial nations and wallets that were under investigation.

Patrick Steven Yaroch, 37, used his investigative positions with access to FBI systems to steal cryptocurrency, then exchange and launder it through financial transactions to conceal its source and ownership.

Yaroch made about 100 fraudulent transfers related to 35 FBI investigations, gaining $2,474,380.66 in proceeds, according to a news release from the US Attorney’s Office, Eastern District of Virginia on Thursday.

He had earlier admitted to unauthorized transfers that involved about $1 million in digital assets, which was reported by Cointelegraph in August.

Yaroch paid off his debts and intended to use the proceeds to finance a new life in Portugal, where he planned to buy a vineyard, according to the statement.

The former agent admitted that his actions had compromised FBI investigations related to criminal groups and adversarial nations.

Yaroch faces up to 20 years in prison. His sentencing is set for March 4, 2027.

#5064. StoneX sees 97% upside for Bullish as $4.2 billion Equiniti deal opens tokenization market

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

StoneX says Bullish's pending $4.2 billion Equiniti acquisition will make it transfer agent of record for about 30% of the S&P 500 and half the FTSE 100.

StoneX Financial on Monday initiated coverage of Bullish with a Buy rating and a $42 price target, arguing that the exchange is set to become a "toll booth" for tokenized shares if its Equiniti acquisition deal follows through.

Bullish shares were trading above $22 in early morning trading, up 2.9% on the day, according to The Block's BLSH price page. StoneX's target implies roughly 97% upside from that level, although the stock is down nearly 50% over six months.

In a note to clients, StoneX analyst Mark Palmer said the Equiniti acquisition will give Bullish a transfer agency business serving nearly 3,000 issuers, 15,000 corporate clients, and 20 million shareholders once completed.

"Every big market structure shift needs a toll booth, and BLSH is buying one," Palmer wrote. "When the deal closes, BLSH will own the transfer agent of record for ~30% of the S&P 500 and half the FTSE 100, thereby commanding the point at which a share stops being a database entry and becomes a token."

The $4.2 billion Equiniti deal includes $1.85 billion in assumed debt and about $2.35 billion in Bullish shares. Equiniti processes $500 billion in annual payments, according to the note.

Palmer said Bullish could capture a bigger share of a market that is projected to grow astronomically in the coming years. Tokenized equities total about $2.1 billion onchain, against $151 trillion in global equity value. The broader tokenized asset market, meanwhile, could reach $9.4 trillion by 2030, according to projections cited in the note.

On revenue, the exchange's existing business provides a solid base, Palmer noted. Bullish's second-quarter adjusted revenue rose 62% year over year to $92.6 million. Its subscription, services and other revenue also reached a record $62.7 million, per the note.

StoneX flagged several risks to its investment thesis, including an unforeseen delay or failure of the Equiniti deal. The firm also pointed to slower tokenization adoption and competition from Nasdaq, DTCC, NYSE, Securitize, and Superstate.

Meanwhile, Bullish appointed former New York Stock Exchange President Stacey Cunningham as its president. The appointment comes as the company awaits regulatory approval for its Equiniti acquisition and expands its issuer-sponsored tokenization business across Solana, Aptos, and Ethereum.

#5061. Ethereum Foundation, Vitalik Buterin sued over firing after ‘suicide pledge’ dispute

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The complaint challenges Fulton’s dismissal and quotes a recorded Buterin conversation about handling internal dissent.

A former Ethereum Foundation employee is suing the organization and members of its leadership, alleging she was unlawfully terminated after challenging an employee mandate.

Kayleen Fulton, known by the moniker "Nixo," filed the complaint on Nov. 4 in Colorado. The defendants include the Ethereum Foundation, its Singapore affiliate, executive Bastian Aue and Ethereum co-founder Vitalik Buterin.

The basis of the lawsuit stems from Fulton's dismissal on April 6 and a dispute over the Foundation's "EF Mandate" that outlines its updated mission and principles. Fulton's suit also alleges disability discrimination and interference with medical leave rights coinciding with a cancer diagnosis she disclosed to the EF around the same time.

The Block reached out to Fulton and the Ethereum Foundation, but did not receive a reply from either before publication.

According to the complaint, employees were given the choice to affirm their commitment to the mandate's direction or leave. However, the filing acknowledges that Aue said the seppuku reference was "not meant to be taken literally" and later clarified that the commitment had "nothing to do with literal seppuku."

Fulton and another colleague, Trent Van Epps, organized a letter endorsing the EF's direction while objecting to some of the conditions of the mandate, which ultimately garnered 53 signatures. The complaint alleges that because of this pushback, Fulton and Van Epps were unlawfully terminated.

The filing also quotes a May 1 conversation with Buterin that Fulton partially recorded. It does not state whether Buterin knew or consented to the conversation being recorded, but notes that it took place in Norway, where one-party consent to recording is legal.

The complaint quotes Buterin as saying that "if you reward making a stink with outcomes," employees could use that as a way to influence the Foundation.

Fulton said she was offered a year's salary under a separation agreement, but alleges she signed the release under duress, received no payments, and later rescinded it.

She is seeking an unspecified amount in damages and lost pay.

#5059. Why a Hyperliquid Treasury Firm Wants to Become a US Futures Broker

Why a Hyperliquid Treasury Firm Wants to Become a US Futures Broker

Hyperliquid Strategies built its business around holding HYPE. Now it wants to help Americans trade on Hyperliquid, too.

The Nasdaq-listed treasury firm, which trades as PURR and is chaired by former Barclays CEO Bob Diamond, disclosed in its quarterly results Thursday that a subsidiary has applied to become a futures commission merchant, or FCM.

That’s the type of CFTC-registered broker that holds customer funds and handles futures orders. If approved, the unit would carry U.S. client accounts for permissioned markets built on Hyperliquid, letting Americans trade perpetual futures within a regulated framework.

Perpetual futures are derivatives with no expiration date that let traders take leveraged positions. Hyperliquid, a major onchain venue for them, still blocks U.S. users.

Its HIP-3 framework lets outside firms launch their own perpetual markets on Hyperliquid’s order book after staking HYPE. A new permissioned version, called HIP-3*, would let a regulated deployer run markets restricted to approved traders, with Hyperliquid acting as neutral infrastructure.

“We deployed $802 million this quarter and filed an application to become a futures commission merchant,” CEO David Schamis said. “Those two moves are the same bet.”

The firm held about 38.5 million HYPE at the end of September, worth roughly $3.5 billion, and more U.S. trading routed through Hyperliquid would likely mean more fees for the protocol, which uses fee revenue to buy back HYPE. An FCM would also give Hyperliquid Strategies a business of its own beyond holding tokens.

During the quarter, the firm spent $785 million buying 9.1 million HYPE and $17 million repurchasing 2.7 million of its own shares, funded largely by $911 million raised through stock sales. It reported $620.6 million in net income, driven almost entirely by $803 million in unrealized gains as HYPE rose from about $65 to $91.

It won’t be alone. Kraken parent Payward has said it plans to deploy HIP-3* markets through its CFTC-regulated Bitnomial exchange, with NinjaTrader Clearing serving as the FCM.

And last week, NYSE owner Intercontinental Exchange agreed to team up with Trade[XYZ] to bring Hyperliquid-based perpetual futures to the U.S., sending HYPE up 13%. Trade[XYZ] already runs markets on Hyperliquid, including perpetual futures tied to the S&P 500.

Regulators must sign off on both the FCM registration and the permissioned HIP-3 markets it would serve, and the company has given no timeline.

Sen. Elizabeth Warren is setting her sights on prediction market oversight and the Trump family's burgeoning crypto businesses when she becomes chair of the Senate Banking Committee in the new year.

Warren, who has been critical of crypto, will be focused on four main areas when she helms the committee in January, after Democrats took control of the Senate last week, including affordability, artificial intelligence, prediction markets, a looming potential financial crisis, and accountability, a Warren banking committee aide said.

"Warren is looking to focus on the issues that have been the focus of her career and drove the election outcomes across the country on Tuesday - making life more affordable for families across the country, reining in industries that are raising costs and undermining the financial security of Americans, and rooting out corruption," the aide said on Monday.

Congressional committee chairs drive the agenda, so Warren will lead efforts involving banks, deposit insurance, housing, the Federal Reserve – and crypto. Maxine Waters are poised to lead, respectively.

In letters, she has asked for an investigation into Trump's memecoin as well as an ethics probe into alleged conflicts tied to Trump-family-backed crypto project World Liberty Financial, the United Arab Emirates, and Trump aides.

"Big Tech CEOs, Administration officials, Trump family crypto businesses, Wall Street executives, and giant corporations should preserve their documents and prepare to answer tough questions," the aide said.

Lawmakers have repeatedly brought up concerns about insider trading over the past year that were then ignited after an active-duty U.S. Army soldier was charged for using confidential information to place bets on the prediction market Polymarket ahead of former Venezuelan President Nicolás Maduro’s capture earlier this year.

In another, a former White House teleprompter agreed to pay more than $172,000 to settle charges that he used his advance access to President Trump's speeches to profit on Kalshi.

"She is looking to end the current Wild West-style lack of oversight in this area, with corrupt insiders profiting," the aide said.

#5055. MARA posts $634 million Q3 net profit on bitcoin gains, sells 13,597 BTC to pay off loans

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

MARA sold 13,597 bitcoin for approximately $1.1 billion after the quarter ended, using $750 million to fully repay its bitcoin-collateralized loans.

MARA Holdings (Nasdaq: MARA) reported a third-quarter net profit of $634.4 million, compared with $123.1 million a year earlier, despite revenue falling 35% year over year to $164.2 million. The firm reported revenue of $174.9 million in the prior quarter.

Profit was driven largely by bitcoin appreciation, with the company recording nearly $885 million in fair-value gains on its digital assets and bitcoin receivables, while mining revenue declined as block reward income fell 37%.

Separately, MARA said it sold 13,597 bitcoin for approximately $1.1 billion after the quarter ended, using $750 million to fully repay its bitcoin-collateralized loans.

Once one of the leading public bitcoin mining companies, MARA has been transitioning into an AI infrastructure provider by expanding its high-performance computing business. It's currently the fifth-largest corporate bitcoin treasury, having been surpassed by Strive in recent months.

"When we think about MARA’s future, the opportunity is bigger than monetizing the megawatt," CEO Fred Thiel wrote in the quarterly shareholder letter. "It is about increasing the value we can capture from that megawatt as we move from power to infrastructure to compute, and ultimately to fully integrated cloud solutions."

MARA is shifting its focus from expanding its infrastructure platform to securing customer leases and generating free cash flow, leveraging its bitcoin mining operations, energy portfolio and partnerships to extract greater value from its power assets. Since the beginning of the year, the firm has reduced its total debt by 37% to $1.4 billion.

"Our responsibility now is to execute with discipline and to show you the returns these assets can produce," Thiel concluded.

MARA's stock was little changed in after-hours trading, after rising more than 4.5% in the regular session.
