#5008. New Camping Gear We’re Testing (and Liking) This Fall

Rainproof apparel, heated pads for the tent, and equipment for the fall camping season.

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?

HELLO, SHOULDER SEASON. Easily one of the best and (sometimes) overlooked times for camping, fall has all kinds of intrigue. From fewer people and colorful foliage to a crispness to the air and the possibility of high-elevation snow, there’s so much to be drawn to.

Sure, the temperature will fall, but that’s no reason to stow away the sleeping bag and tent for the year. Instead, add a few pieces of gear here and there to set yourself up for the best fall camping outing imaginable. That could be anything from a proper car camping mattress to another layer of apparel. Of course, a little waterproof gear and some added light-producers will only help your cause.

So, keep that camp stove within reach as we slip into November and December. There’s a decent chance your favorite campsite is still open and better still, not subject to a waiting list.

Ourselves, we’ve still been sleeping in the outdoors and trekking on trails—it’s a year-round pursuit of testing gear for us. With the change of the season, we wanted to highlight 15 new items we’re liking as we settle into the fall camping season, all tested in-house to make sure they’re worthy of your hard-earned cash and time.

As fall sets in, it’s good to fortify. That means protecting your belongings with another weatherproof layer. A waterproof soft cooler like this is a great way to do so as it functions more like a spacious messenger bag ready for any weather forecast. I’ve been taking mine out on the river to fly-fish or slinging it over my back for a bike commute, even in the rain. The shoulder strap is uber-comfortable, the extra webbing loops are great for attaching more gear (per a longer camping trip), and the insulation is impressive. It loads and unloads efficiently and the 14 liters of volume is just about ideal for the day-long ventures. Pack some cans on ice for the beach or throw your valuables and a change of clothes in there for a day on the raft.

The latest from Nestout is a savvy little heater built for warming bleachers, camp chairs, sleeping bags, and more. It’s a cool-weather camping centerpiece, delivering an impressive 60W of power and tested to 32 degrees F before being released to the wild. The topography-themed pattern is a nice touch, as is the efficiency of the thing, which powers up way faster than the vast majority of electric blankets we’ve tried—not to mention it produces more heat. Go with the BOOST mode for a speedy warm-me-up or the SLEEP setting for consistent comfortable temperatures as you settle in for the night. It rolls up to about the size of a sub sandwich when not in use, making it road-ready. And we love how easy it is to pull off the removable cover when it’s time for a quick hand wash. Note the higher power intake as you plan your next weekend trip as some standard car outlets and similar may not make the cut.

While they can be a bit tricky to get on, once they’re set, they’re going to be the difference between a comfortable SUP paddle on a chilly evening and one that involves chattering teeth and a premature ending. #5007. BTC price eyes $73K amid Nvidia volatility: Three things to know in Bitcoin this week

Bitcoin price action fell to its lowest levels since mid-August as Nvidia reports added to sell-side pressure across stock-market futures.

Bitcoin (BTC) fell to about $73,500 late Sunday, its lowest level since August, as more than $1 billion in leveraged crypto positions were liquidated ahead of this week’s US inflation reports.

Bitcoin slipped below $74,000 shortly before Sunday’s weekly close, extending last week’s losses, TradingView data showed.

BTC/USD one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

About $1.12 billion in leveraged crypto positions were liquidated in the 24 hours through 9 am UTC Monday, according to CoinGlass.

Crypto liquidations over 24 hours. Source: CoinGlass

Bitcoin fell below its 50-week simple moving average (SMA) of $75,492 and approached its 21-week SMA of $73,078.

BTC/USD one-day chart with 21-week and 50-week SMAs. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Bitcoin also traded below the short-term holder cost basis, which CryptoQuant put at $75,691 on Monday. The metric estimates the average cost basis of coins that last moved less than 155 days ago.

#5006. BMW is going upscale with its first 3-row EV, the iX7

BMW’s next electric SUV won’t just be bigger. The iX7 will sit at the top of the brand’s EV lineup when it debuts in 2027, bringing a more luxurious option to the mix.

After the new X5 and iX5, the X7, BMW’s biggest SUV, is due for a Neue Klasse overhaul. BMW said the second-generation X7 will arrive in 2027, including the first fully electric version, the iX7.

Ahead of its upcoming debut, camouflaged prototypes have been spotted testing, revealing a few design changes.

Although it won’t fully adopt BMW’s Neue Klasse design, like the iX3, the three-row SUV appears to feature a slimmer, more upright Kidney Grille instead of the traditional wide BMW Grille.

The iX7 also swaps conventional pull-style door handles for small handles on the vehicle’s pillar. It also retains split headlights rather than the updated Neue Klasse-style headlights on the iX3 (shown below on the right).

While BMW has yet to reveal details, a Reddit user who claims to work at BMW’s factory in South Carolina and has seen the second-generation X7 shared a few new details.

Like the X5 and iX5, the new X7 and iX7 will retain a more traditional design rather than going full-on Neue Klasse, including the Kidney Grille. The user also said BMW is dropping the split tailgate, which it did with the new X5, in favor of a standard one-piece tailgate.

Inside, the dashboard and center console are similar to the new X5, but with upgraded tech and features from the new 7 Series sedan.

BMW said the new 7 Series sedan “takes the lead when it comes to the introduction of technologies from the Neue Klasse into existing models.”

Optional features will reportedly include the upgraded 31.3″ 8K BMW Theatre Screen for rear passengers and a 14.6″ BMW Passenger Screen. It will also have optional touch screen panels on the rear doors, the user said.

While most of the claims make sense, we’ll have to wait for BMW to confirm it officially. The new X7 and iX7 are expected to debut in mid-2027.

Like the iX5 60 xDrive, the three-row iX7 is expected to use a dual-motor all-wheel-drive powertrain. The iX5’s AWD setup delivers a combined 578 hp (425 kW) and 593 lb-ft of torque, enabling it to hit 0 to 60 mph in 4.4 seconds.

The iX5 uses a massive 144 kWh battery, which BMW said offers up to 435 miles of range. It will also use an 800V electrical system for fast charging.

BMW showed just how fast it can charge during a media event in Southern California earlier this month, where the iX5 60 xDrive hit a peak of 464 kW. With up to 460 kW peak charging, the iX5 60 xDrive can add up to 170 miles of range in 10 minutes, BMW says.

The new X7 and iX7 are expected to go into production at BMW’s Spartanburg plant in SC in late 2027, joining the new iX5.

#5005. NYSE Taps Avalanche to Settle Its Planned Tokenized Stock Platform

NYSE Taps Avalanche to Settle Its Planned Tokenized Stock Platform

The New York Stock Exchange is turning to Avalanche to power the behind-the-scenes plumbing of its forthcoming tokenized securities platform, one of the clearest signs yet that Wall Street’s oldest institutions are moving core market infrastructure onto public blockchains.

Ava Labs, the company behind Avalanche, said Monday that the blockchain will serve as the “core ledger technology” for the clearing firm behind an NYSE-affiliated digital trading venue.

The platform, which the NYSE’s parent Intercontinental Exchange previously said it was developing, is designed to allow 24/7 trading, clearing and settlement of tokenized securities, subject to regulatory approval. Running on Avalanche, it aims to bring the clearing of tokenized equities onchain at institutional scale.

Tokenization, the practice of issuing traditional assets as blockchain-based tokens, has become one of Wall Street’s buzziest pursuits, but most efforts so far have centered on individual products rather than the market machinery itself.

The NYSE’s move is notable because it puts a blockchain underneath the clearing and settlement layer, the plumbing that finalizes trades, rather than just wrapping a single fund or stock in a token.

Dennis Lee, the NYSE’s head of digital issuance, said the exchange is “picking the right technology for each job,” and that while its planned platform will support equities listed across a range of blockchains, the ledger recording and settling trades needs to be fast, reliable and able to control who participates.

Emin Gün Sirer, Ava Labs’ CEO and founder, framed the deal as proof that blockchains can support not just products but the infrastructure they trade on, citing Avalanche’s “24/7 atomic settlement” and predictable costs.

The announcement adds to a frenetic race to put stocks and their underlying infrastructure onchain.

The NYSE separately tapped Blockchain.com to reach crypto investors with tokenized stocks and ETFs, while ICE and crypto exchange OKX are planning a 24/7 tokenized stock venue under an SEC exemption, and Morgan Stanley has moved to tokenize securities.

That OKX-ICE effort has hit friction, though: Every Magnificent Seven stock, including Apple and Meta, opted out of having its shares tokenized on the venue, with 67 companies in all joining them.

Research shows the appetite for tokenized stocks is real but uneven. An IMF study found tokenized stocks show genuine demand yet remain volatile and illiquid, a reminder that the infrastructure may be arriving faster than the markets to fill it.

#5003. Waymo adds nearly 250 square miles of robotaxi coverage in 5 cities

Waymo is growing its driverless service area by nearly 250 square miles over the next week, spread across Los Angeles, Nashville, Orlando, San Diego, and the San Francisco Bay Area.

That’s about as big as Tesla’s entire Houston Robotaxi geofence after this week’s expansion, and Waymo is adding it in cities where it already runs with nobody in the driver’s seat.

The company announced the expansion in a short post on its blog today. Here’s what changes in each market, with Waymo’s updated service area maps:

Service now runs down the coast into the South Bay, through Manhattan Beach and Redondo Beach to Torrance.

The zone pushes further south, and riders can now get to the zoo.

Coverage on the east side nearly doubles, and there’s more service downtown.

The service area now stretches north to La Jolla, taking in University City and Clairemont along the way.

Waymo is pushing further into the South Bay, including a connection to BART at Berryessa station in San Jose. The service area now runs from San Francisco down the Peninsula to San Jose and Campbell.

Waymo didn’t break out square mileage by city, and it didn’t say how many vehicles it’s adding to cover the new ground. The updated maps are already in the Waymo app.

Some of these markets are very young. Waymo opened Nashville to public riders in April with a zone of about 60 square miles covering downtown, Midtown, Music Row, and East Nashville. San Diego only went public on September 1, with roughly 50 square miles between the beach and I-15 that barely crossed north of I-8. So getting to La Jolla less than two months later is a big jump.

In May, Waymo pushed its coverage past 1,400 square miles across 11 cities, which is bigger than the state of Rhode Island. Since then, it went driverless in Las Vegas, launched San Diego, and now says it operates in 15 US metros with more than 500,000 paid rides per week. It’s even mapping Toronto for a 2028 launch.

Tesla is also drawing bigger maps. After launching Robotaxi in Dallas and Houston with tiny geofences in April, it stretched Houston to about 254 square miles this week, according to Robotaxi Tracker. But the tracker counts 34 vehicles in Houston.

I'd like to see Waymo add more airports to their service list. Airports are huge drivers of both ride count and revenue for ride services. Waymo currently serves airports in some of their cities, such as Phoenix, San Francisco, and Nashville. However, other major airports such as LAX, Atlanta, and Dallas/Fort Worth do not currently have terminal service even though Waymo operates in those cities. Adding these might not look flashy on a map, but it would be a huge addition to Waymo's bottom line.

And on the metric that reflects actual use, it’s not close. Waymo logged 3.9 million rider-only miles per week in Q2 2026, while Tesla’s driverless fleet did 558,000 miles in its latest reported week, mostly in Austin. That’s about 7x, and it comes on the same day Elon Musk claimed the robotaxi race against Waymo “was never a real contest.”

I like this. Good progress. But I’ll be truly impressed when the Bay Area service area extends south into LA, expands north, and the same happens in San Diego.

It’s going to happen. Waymo is already dabbling with highway operations in California.I can see them linking all those service areas within the next 2 years. Maybe even by the end of next year if all goes well.

#5001. Tom Lee’s Bitmine Adds $20M in Ethereum, Seizing on Vitalik’s 'ETH Is Money' Declaration

Tom Lee’s Bitmine Adds $20M in Ethereum, Seizing on Vitalik’s 'ETH Is Money' Declaration

Bitmine Immersion Technologies extended its weekly Ethereum buying streak, adding 9,587 ETH worth roughly $20 million, as chairman Tom Lee pointed to a recent declaration by Ethereum co-founder Vitalik Buterin as validation of the firm’s accumulation strategy.

The NYSE-listed company said Monday its holdings reached 6,070,092 ETH, worth roughly $13.6 billion at a reference price of $2,234 per coin.

That’s 4.97% of Ethereum’s 122.2 million token supply, leaving Bitmine, by its own count, 99% of the way to its goal of owning 5% of the network, a target it calls the “Alchemy of 5%,” reached in just 15 months.

The firm has bought Ethereum every week since launching its treasury strategy on June 30, 2025.

Lee leaned heavily on comments Buterin made at the Devcon conference in Mumbai, where the Ethereum founder said, “I’ve finally made up my mind, ETH is a store of value, ETH is money.” Lee called the declaration “a seismic shift,” arguing it clarifies how ETH will capture value in what he described as a coming “supercycle of tokenization and AI.” (Disclosure: Tom Lee is one of several investors in Dastan, Decrypt’s parent company.)

He contended ETH functions as “pristine and trusted collateral” rather than a cash-flow token, reflecting its position as the layer-1 network favored by financial institutions moving assets onchain.

Buterin elaborated in an X post, laying out why he now sees ETH primarily as a store of value, including his view that fee-burning is weaker than it once seemed and that optimizing for it can conflict with decentralization. Lee framed that reasoning as consistent with Ethereum’s roadmap and its role in the tokenization wave.

Counting cash, other tokens and “moonshot” investments, Bitmine’s total holdings stood at $14.3 billion as of Nov. 8, including 216 Bitcoin, $475 million in cash and marketable securities, a $180 million stake in Beast Industries and a $75 million position in Eightco Holdings, which it bills as a rare public proxy for indirect OpenAI exposure.

Bitmine remains the world’s largest Ethereum treasury and the second-largest crypto treasury overall, trailing only Michael Saylor’s Strategy, which the release said holds about 849,327 BTC. Bitmine has 5,227,037 ETH, roughly 86% of its holdings, staked through its MAVAN platform, with projected annualized staking revenue of about $359 million.

The firm also recently argued the muted market reaction to the record Binance hack showed crypto’s growing resilience.

#5000. Rivian electric van spotted in Walmart+ livery, hinting at a fleet deal

A Rivian electric delivery van wrapped in Walmart+ branding was photographed on a car hauler on I-94 in northwest Indiana today. It’s the first public sign that Walmart could become Rivian’s next big van customer after Amazon.

Neither company has announced a deal. But Walmart’s last two electric van suppliers are out of the business, and Rivian is one of the few companies still building a purpose-built electric delivery van in the US.

Reddit user biggie_ballzzz posted the photo to r/RIVNstock this afternoon, asking if anyone had heard about a partnership. The van is strapped to a multi-car trailer between two Ford pickups, with a big “Walmart+” logo on the side and “Same day. Right to your door.” on the rear doors.

There’s no mistaking the vehicle. It’s Rivian’s Commercial Van, with the same tall body and red light bar wrapping around the rear that you see on Amazon Prime vans everywhere.

A few commenters suspected an AI image, but there’s no clear evidence. In fact, a second commenter then shared a photo of a van in the same livery parked in front of a Walmart Supercenter in Hammond, Indiana, about 30 miles to the west.

Rivian (RIVN) stock was up more than 3% this afternoon as the photo made the rounds, according to screenshots shared in the thread. That’s two days before Rivian reports its Q3 2026 results on Thursday, October 29.

Walmart has been trying to electrify last-mile delivery for years. Its supplier picks haven’t aged well.

In 2022, it signed up to buy 4,500 Canoo vans and put them to work on InHome deliveries. Canoo filed for Chapter 7 bankruptcy in January 2025 and ceased operations.

The same year, Walmart reserved 5,000 BrightDrop vans from GM, and officially added them to its delivery fleet in late 2024. Then GM killed BrightDrop production at its CAMI plant in Ontario a year ago, with no plan to build the van anywhere else. BrightDrop was on pace for roughly 4,000 US sales in 2025, compared to more than 60,000 for GM’s gas-powered Express and Savana vans.

So Walmart is running two orphaned EV van platforms. And if it wants more purpose-built electric vans at scale, the list of US options is very short.

Amazon ordered 100,000 vans from Rivian, and its fleet has now passed 40,000 units on the road. But the deal came with an exclusivity period that ended in November 2023, and Rivian has struggled to land anything close to Amazon-scale since.

AT&T signed up for a pilot in late 2023, and HelloFresh came on board in 2025. In February 2025, Rivian opened van sales to any business, with the RCV 500 starting at $79,900 and the larger RCV 700 at $83,900. None of those customers moves the needle like Walmart would.

There’s also a charging angle. Walmart now operates its own 400 kW fast-charging network at 100 stores in 20 states, and its manager of retail charging, Wade Higgins, previously worked at Rivian, according to his bio on the board of Minnesota car-sharing nonprofit HOURCAR.

One van on a trailer isn’t a fleet order. Not yet, anyway. This could be a test unit for a pilot program, like the one AT&T announced in 2023. And since Rivian sells vans to any business now, Walmart could have simply bought a few to try them out.

So Walmart is running two orphaned EV van platforms. And if it wants more purpose-built electric vans at scale, the list of US options is very short.

Walmart also runs Ford E-Transit vans but I can't find recent numbers for how many

But the timing makes sense. Walmart bet on Canoo and GM, and both left it with vans nobody builds anymore. If you’re Walmart and you want to keep electrifying same-day delivery, Rivian is the obvious call. Its plant in Normal, Illinois, has already built tens of thousands of these vans, and the platform has been proven at scale by the one company Walmart is trying to beat on delivery.

That’s the awkward part. Walmart putting its brand on the same van as Amazon Prime is a bit funny, and it says a lot about how thin the electric van market has gotten in the US.

For Rivian, a Walmart deal would matter. The van business gives it volume in Normal while R2 ramps, and it badly needs proof that the van isn’t just an Amazon product. We’ll see if Rivian says anything on Thursday’s call. We wouldn’t bet on a massive order announcement, but a Walmart pilot wouldn’t surprise us one bit.

#4999. How to Build Your Legs With the Glute-Ham Raise

This underrated exercise could be the MVP of your lower body training.

IF YOU’RE A dedicated lifter, your lower body doesn’t lack attention in your workouts. You might even dedicate an entire session to it each week (hello, leg day). But just because your training plan is packed with deadlifts, squats, lunges, calf raises, hip thrusts, and step-ups doesn’t mean you’re doing everything you can to maximize your leg development. You probably walk right past one of the most powerful lower body machines every time you’re at the gym: the glute ham developer (GHD).

It might look like a gussied-up horizontal Roman chair, but the GHD is a different beast entirely. Instead of targeting your glutes and lower back with hyperextensions, the GHD allows you to laser focus on your biceps femoris, semitendinosus, and semimembranosus (collectively known as your hamstrings) with an exercise called the glute ham raise (GHR). It’s so effective at what it does that only the deadlift can rival its hamstring-building power, making it a formidable addition to any leg day. Use these tips to weave it into your routine for maximum results.

AS MENTIONED ABOVE, you’ll need a GHD to perform a GHR. Where a Roman chair puts your body at a 45-degree angle and only allows movement in the hips, the GHD elevates you off the floor, locking your ankles between two sets of rollers while your knees (in the starting position) and then thighs (in the down position) rest on rounded pads. In so doing, it shifts the emphasis from your lower back to the backs of your legs, requiring you to use your hamstrings to lower and raise your body primarily through knee flexion as you perform the GHR.

How well does it work? A small research trial suggests the glute ham raise is so effective at targeting your hamstrings that it’s on par with the Romanian deadlift and superior to such leg-sculpting staples as the leg curl and good morning.

And the benefits don’t stop there. In addition to your hams, you’ll light up muscles all along your posterior chain, including your calves, glutes, and erector spinae. Even your abs will kick into gear to help your erector spinae support and stabilize your spine.

IF YOUR STRENGTH program is divided into training splits, add the GHR to your leg day. If your weekly routine skews more toward total body workouts, incorporate the GHR into one or two of them, making sure it’s among a diverse selection of leg exercises spread across three or four strength sessions. Either way, it’s a good idea to perform it toward the end of a workout after heavier compound lifts like the squat and deadlift.

You can load the GHD by holding a weight plate to your chest, but start with only your bodyweight, following the instructions below until you master the movement.

In a true GHR, the lowering phase of the movement stops when your body is horizontal to the floor. Going past that point by hinging downward at your hips until your torso is perpendicular to the floor adds a back extension element and causes you to lose hamstring tension, undercutting your hamstring gains—so make sure you stay disciplined with your range of motion.

*If you don’t have access to a GHD, you can enlist a buddy to hold your ankles on the floor or use a specialized bench to perform a Nordic curl, which also requires your hamstrings to work overtime as you lower and raise your thighs and torso through knee flexion.

Trevor Thieme is a Los Angeles-based writer and strength coach, and a former fitness editor at Men’s Health. When not helping others get in shape, he splits his time between surfing, skiing, hiking, mountain biking, and trying to keep up with his 10-year-old daughter.

#4998. Robinhood opens access to US IPOs for UK retail investors

Robinhood’s UK customers can request shares in eligible US initial public offerings at the offer price before the stocks begin trading.

Robinhood has launched a service that lets UK retail investors request shares in eligible US initial public offerings (IPOs) before trading begins.

#4997. Coinbase’s US national trust bank charter takes effect

Coinbase plans to expand its institutional crypto custody business under the federal charter and is exploring digital asset payment products.

Coinbase’s national trust bank charter took effect on Friday, according to the US Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC).

The applied for the charter in October 2025 and received preliminary approval in April 2026, according to the OCC’s filing record. The charter allows Coinbase National Trust Company to hold digital assets on behalf of clients as a fiduciary, according to the OCC’s preliminary approval letter.

Coinbase plans to offer institutional clients segregated custody accounts, with access to staking, financing and trading through other Coinbase entities, according to its charter application.

Coinbase’s application also outlined plans to explore digital asset payment products and custody services that would allow clients to interact with select smart contracts.

Other crypto companies that received OCC national trust bank charters include Ripple, Circle, BitGo, Fidelity Digital Assets and Paxos.

Crypto.com and Stripe-owned Bridge received conditional approval, while Kraken and EDX Markets are still pursuing charters.

#4996. BYD’s $10,000 EV blows a tire at nearly 90 mph — Watch what happens

BYD updated its lowest-priced electric car, the Seagull, which starts at around $10,000 in China, with a few extras, including a high-speed tire blowout system that stabilizes the vehicle at nearly 90 mph.

If the $10,000 price tag wasn’t enough, BYD is giving buyers even more reason to check out its entry-level electric hatch.

The Seagull is among BYD’s most popular vehicles, sold as the Dolphin Surf in Europe and the UK and as the Dolphin Mini in other global markets.

BYD launched the second-generation Seagull EV last week, starting at just 62,900 yuan, or about $9,500. That’s including BYD’s limited-time Super Trade-In incentive.

Regular prices start at 65,900 yuan ($9,900), which is still less than its predecessor. The first-generation Seagull EV started at 69,900 yuan ($10,500) without incentives, making it BYD’s most affordable purely electric vehicle in China.

For its second generation, the Seagull is bigger, more advanced, and adds a bit more luxury to the entry-level segment.

The new BYD Seagull is 4,205 mm long, 1,810 mm wide, and 1,570 mm high, with a 2,650 mm wheelbase. That’s 425 mm longer and has a 150 mm longer wheelbase than the previous model.

Thanks to the added space, BYD’s electric hatch now offers room for five passengers, up from four seats that the first-gen Seagull had.

BYD added several new features with its latest tech, including a bigger 15.6″ infotainment screen with its DiLink 150 smart cockpit system and a “140 km/h high-speed tire blowout stability system” as standard.

To showcase how the tire blowout system works, BYD put the updated electric hatch to the test at full speed.

BYD’s general manager of the Ocean Series, Zhang Zhuo, shared a video of the second-generation Seagull EV handling the blowout at 140 km/h, or about 87 mph, like it’s nothing.

The high-speed tire-blowout system lets the Seagull stay in its lane until it comes to a complete stop. Pretty impressive for an entry-level vehicle.

As you can see in the video, the new version also has an available roof-mounted LiDAR. Buyers can upgrade to the optional DiPilot 300 system with BYD’s God’s Eye intelligent driving assistance. The ADAS system provides features like City Navigation on Autopilot (CNOA) and smart parking.

BYD added a few premium extras, including heating, ventilation, and a massage function to the front seats, plus ambient lighting, smart storage options, and a 50W wireless phone charger.

The new Seagull uses BYD’s Blade Batteries 2.0 and Flash Charging Technology. As such, the battery can charge from 10% to 70% in five minutes. A full recharge, or 10% to 97%, takes just nine minutes. Even in temperatures as low as -30°C, it takes 12 minutes to charge from 20% to 97%.

It’s available with two LFP battery packs: 30.1 kWh and 39.2 kWh, providing CLTC ranges of 320 km (199 miles) and 420 km (261 miles), respectively.

To improve the ride quality and handling, BYD upgraded the rear suspension to a multi-link independent system.

The second-gen Seagull also switches to front-wheel-drive (FWD) with a more powerful 85 kW (114 hp) or 95 kW (127 hp) electric motor, up from the 55 kW (75 hp) that its predecessor offered.

BYD’s new Seagull is available in five variants, priced from 65,900 yuan to 99,900 yuan ($9,900 to $15,000), without a trade-in.

#4995. The Best Under-the-Radar Menswear Brands, According to Style Editors

Think of this as your shortcut to discovering your next favorite label.

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?

PERSONALLY, I LOVE discovering new men’s clothing brands because shopping at the same ole’ places time and time again can become redundant. I know what to expect, and sometimes, the latest collections are overhyped and don’t quite deliver. So when I’m scrolling through my social media feed and come across an ad about an emerging label or one that’s flown under the radar, I’m immediately clicking through to see what they’re about. I mean, who doesn’t love finding a brand before everyone else catches on? Before I know it, I’m going down a rabbit hole, browsing the entire site and adding things to my wishlist.

But as a style editor and menswear enthusiast, I’m not just looking for something new for the sake of it. I want to know: Does the brand offer something different from what I already own? Does it bring a fresh perspective to menswear? And, most importantly, is it worth spending my money on? The brands that have really caught my attention tend to check those boxes. So, if you’re looking to switch up your wardrobe and discover something beyond your usual rotation, these are the men’s clothing brands worth knowing about.

Octobre Editions is a French menswear label, founded in Paris in 2016. The brand makes quality everyday clothes that you can wear for years and won’t go out of style. Think fine sweaters, shirts, jackets and coats, sneakers, and sometimes a limited-edition collection.

If you want to look sharp without defaulting to a suit, Reiss is worth a look. The London-based brand specializes in making muted tones of sharp-cut blazers, merino-wool sweater polos, button-downs, and suede jackets that make it easy to dress for the office or dinner.

A great pair of jeans can carry an entire outfit; California-label PAIGE gives you plenty of high-end premium options to find your fit. From slimmer cuts to more relaxed silhouettes, the brand offers denim in a range of washes that play well with everything from a basic tee to a leather jacket. Also in the lineup are classic chinos, sweatshirts, and shackets.

Want to experiment with barrel-leg pants, roomier jackets, or other statement silhouettes without spending a fortune? GU—Uniqlo’s sister company—is worth browsing for affordable everyday pieces that are trend-forward with a unique Tokyo-inspired aesthetic.

Beyond Yoga is a premium, body-inclusive active lifestyle and athleisure brand that produces workout gear that feels just as good outside the gym. Among its offerings are ultra-soft, stretchy workout shirts and tanks, athletic shorts, sweatpants, and hoodies, designed to keep you comfortable whether you’re playing tennis or running errands.

NN.07, an exclusive menswear-only Copenhagen brand, makes clothes that are easy to mix and match, which comes in handy when you want more outfit options without buying an entirely new wardrobe. From knitwear and shirts to trousers and outerwear, its pieces can work across casual plans and occasions that call for a slightly dressier look.

Did you know that Aritzia makes men’s clothing, too? The Canadian fashion design house has introduced cozy sweats and outerwear designed to handle cold temperatures and unpredictable weather. Its puffer jackets and rain jackets are worth a look if you need functional layers for chilly commutes or weekend hikes when the forecast isn't cooperating.

California Arts takes familiar menswear staples and gives them a different point of view through unexpected cuts and proportions. Take its Henleys, which feature an extra-deep button placket or its outerwear, which reworks classic coats with oversized shapes, dramatic lengths, and distinctive details like belted closures. Despite the unconventional silhouettes, many of the pieces can still work with wardrobe staples like straight-leg jeans and leather shoes.

#4993. Vitalik Buterin Moves On From ‘Ultrasound Money,’ Says ETH’s Value Is Stability

Vitalik Buterin Moves On From ‘Ultrasound Money,’ Says ETH’s Value Is Stability

Ethereum co-founder Vitalik Buterin wants people to stop valuing ETH as a fee-burning token and start treating it as a store of value, arguing that the burn was "never" as strong as it seemed. Binji Pande, founding member of Ethlabs, told Decrypt that he agrees, claiming that it is a more "future-proof store of value" than Bitcoin.

It's a notable shift in messaging from the Ethereum community, after ETH was branded as "ultrasound money" when the network started burning a portion of every transaction fee in 2021. The argument was that as more people used Ethereum, fewer coins would exist in circulation, and fewer coins meant a higher price. Now, however, Buterin claims that isn't as important as people may think.

"We should not feel sad if fee burn is < 10% of breakeven indefinitely; the value of ETH comes from elsewhere," Buterin tweeted on Sunday. That value, he argued, is stability, which comes from Ethereum being decentralized, resistant to censorship, and having strong security.

Expanding on why I believe "ETH as store of value (SoV)" makes much more sense to focus on than "ETH as fee-burning token".

1. ETH as fee-burning token never was as strong as it seemed2. Optimizing for ETH as fee-burning token would demand Ethereum and…

— vitalik.eth (@VitalikButerin) November 8, 2026

Those in favour of the fee-burning structure argued that fees gave ETH a price floor, much like a company's stock may be backed by revenue and profits. From 2021 to 2023, Ethereum burned millions of ETH every year, but Buterin said this was "never a true floor." Most of these burns, he said, came from internal crypto uses like lending and silly NFT purchases.

Connor Howe, co-founder and CEO of infrastructure provider Enso, told Decrypt that Ethereum's layer-2 expansion made the fee-burning argument worse. Connor said that a significant portion of activity has moved onto layer-2s, and users pay "almost nothing" to settle transactions. To optimize a fee-burning token, he added, you'd also want to optimise for higher gas prices—which no one wants.

"If we decide we actually want to optimize ETH for burning fees to justify a present-day ~$270B market cap, then Ethereum would essentially be functioning as a world-scale major corporation," Buterin said. In doing that, Ethereum would lose one of its major selling points: decentralization.

"I agree with Vitalik, and I also believe that ETH is best positioned as a future-proof store of value," Ethlabs' Pande told Decrypt. He highlighted that the network is censorship-resistant, that it has "great distribution," is widely available, and that it is "at the center of future growing sectors such as tokenization, Stablecoins, and DeFi."

A store of value is an asset that holds its worth over time, meaning investors can put their money into it without fearing it will disappear. Historically, gold has been seen as the ultimate store of value, and Bitcoin supporters have pitched it as a "digital gold."

Pande, however, argues that both fall short compared to Ethereum.

"Gold has held the SoV role for a long time, but given the world we live in now is much more globalized, we need increased portability, something gold simply cannot offer," Pande told Decrypt. "Storing gold is simply not useful either past a certain point; you reach physical limits."

When it comes to Bitcoin as a store of value, Pande expressed concern that it isn't "future-proof" as AI and quantum threats loom.

Ethereum has been preparing for those threats for a while. In January, the Ethereum Foundation formed a dedicated post-quantum team and, more recently, researchers have debated the potential AI-powered threats. By contrast, the Bitcoin community appears more fractured on how to tackle the quantum threat.

"Security is ingrained more deeply into Ethereum’s culture than anywhere else," Julian Ma, co-founder of Ethlabs, told Decrypt. "AI being used for cyberattacks and potentially breaking cryptography strengthens ETH's position as a programmable store-of-value asset because the years of preparation and large investments into security and formal verification of the Ethereum protocol and its applications are about to pay off."

Pande added that newer assets can’t compete with ETH as a store of value, because there’s no way to verify they’re fairly distributed. The Ethereum Foundation owns less than 0.2% of ETH, he said, and the token has traded for 11 years.

“Trying to distribute or mine a new asset as a SoV in the lord’s 2026 is like trying to hide a sack of meat in a sea of piranhas,” he said. “It can and WILL get centralized.”

Not everyone thinks Ethereum’s leadership got here quickly enough, though. Howe said the Ethereum Foundation “needs to start to actually listen and look at the applications building on top of their network.”

“I think he is right,” Howe said of Buterin's recent tweet, “and I think it took too long to say it.”

#4992. Solana Set for Another Tokenized Stock Venue as Formation Notifies the SEC

Solana Set for Another Tokenized Stock Venue as Formation Notifies the SEC

A new firm is lining up to become one of the first to trade tokenized U.S. stocks onchain, notifying the Securities and Exchange Commission that it intends to run a tokenized securities venue on Solana.

Formation Securities said Monday it had filed the notice to operate under the SEC’s “Innovation Exemption,” the conditional relief the agency issued in September that lets eligible tokenized stocks trade through permissioned automated market maker pools on qualifying venues.

Trading is expected to begin in early 2027, with tokenized shares of Exodus Movement, Forward Industries and Galaxy Digital anticipated at launch.

The pitch is to deliver the benefits of blockchain trading, 24/7 markets and near-instant settlement, without stripping away the rights investors expect from real stock.

Under the exemption, eligible tokens must carry the same voting and dividend rights as traditional shares, and each trade settles atomically onchain, a feature meant to cut settlement risk. A company’s transfer agent still keeps the official shareholder record, while every trade posts to the public ledger in real time.

“Tokenized stocks will succeed only if public companies see the value in them, and we intend to build this market together with issuers,” said Christopher Montagano, president of Formation Securities, which licenses its trading technology from Solana-based exchange Orca.

Tokenization firms Securitize and Superstate are expected to supply tokenized shares, with Securitize CEO Carlos Domingo noting its tokens are each backed by a real share held by its broker-dealer.

Access will be limited to verified investors, who can reach the venue through Formation’s platform and distributors including Alpaca, CoinList’s Passage, custodians Anchorage, BitGo and Fireblocks, and wallet providers Solflare and Squads.

The move adds another entrant to a fast-crowding race to put stocks onchain. The NYSE recently tapped Solana rival Avalanche to settle its own planned tokenized securities platform, Securitize launched tokenized stocks on Solana, and an OKX-ICE venue is also in the works, though every Magnificent Seven stock opted out of that one.

The effort builds on Project Open, a proposal a coalition including Orca, Superstate and the Solana Policy Institute submitted to the SEC in 2025.

Formation chose Solana, it said, for its speed, low costs and track record with tokenized assets, noting the network hosts more than $5 billion in tokenized real-world assets.

#4991. The Best Fundamental Kettlebell Exercises for Beginners to Master

These are the cornerstone movements you need to train with these useful gym tools.

SOME OF THE most powerful, effectivekettlebell exercises rely on a few basic movements. Follow our practice recommendations to familiarize yourself with these building blocks, and they’ll become second nature in no time. Once you’ve mastered these, you’ll be able to tackle the more complex stuff—and supersize your gains.

These are the types of movements you'll learn in No Gym Required: Kettlebells, an MH training guide. You'll learn everything from the fundamentals for beginners to more advanced techniques and workout plans.

Want to get a jumpstart on your kettlebell practice? Men's Health MVP Premium members have exclusive access to a 14-day training program straight from the book, so you can kick off your workouts with expert guidance. Here's how you can get it.

Why: This move teaches you the hip hinge, which is an essential movement pattern that’s instrumental in making you powerful—and just may help you avoid lower back pain too. You should hinge whenever you bend to pick something up from the floor, pushing your butt back while keeping your lower back flat and your torso rigid. This protects your lower back while also setting you up to transfer force efficiently from the floor to your upper body. It’s key in kettlebell training: Whenever you deadlift, clean, snatch, squat, or row correctly, you’re also hinging. Take it slowly as you learn it. Later, you’ll be able to generate maximum power whether you’re holding a kettlebell, swinging a golf club, or punching a heavy bag.

Key Performance Points: Bend at the waist and not at the knees. Instead of visualizing your chest going down and up, focus on pushing your butt backward, then bring it forward. The closer you can keep the kettlebell to your body, the more effective these moves will be.

Sets and Reps: Do 3 sets of 10 to 12 reps. Focus on feeling your glutes. Rest 60 seconds between each set.

Why: The swing is one of the most popular and effective kettlebell moves, so mastering the finer points of the form is a must for anyone looking to add the implement to their repertoire. The key here is making sure to emphasize the hip hinge—when you lower the weight down, be sure not to squat before thrusting to snap the weight up. The hips are among your body’s most powerful joints. They’re also among the most mobile. Learning to contract your glutes and extend your hip joints forcefully is foundational to just about any athletic movement you can think of, and it all starts with the swing.

Key Performance Point: Don’t use your shoulders to drive the bell upward. Think of your arms like rope—they just attach your body to the bell. Your legs will create the power to swing upward. Focus on glute drive. The faster and more powerfully you stand and squeeze your glutes, the more easily the bell will swing upward. Keep your abs super-tight. That will protect your spine as the bell swings backward on each rep.

Sets and Reps: Aim to do 10 to 15 reps in 45 seconds, then rest for 15 seconds. Repeat for 5 sets.

Why: What happens when you combine a hip hinge and a front rack? It’s a little more complicated than just putting one and two together, but you’ll finish with a kettlebell clean. As we mentioned above, the hinge transfers power from the floor to your upper body with maximal efficiency. Cleaning the kettlebell is the next logical step: After you propel the bell upward with your glutes and hamstrings, your shoulder and forearm take over as you roll your wrist around and catch the bell in the rack position. Back in the days of the old-time strongmen, a lift wasn’t complete unless you moved a heavy object from the floor to overhead. The clean gets you most of the way there.

Key Performance Points: Thumb to chest. If you feel the kettlebell slamming into you, guide it more. Try a drill: Stand facing a wall and perform cleans. This will remind you not to swing the kettlebell out in front of you. Keep your thumb pointed in. This facilitates the roll around the wrist of the kettlebell into the rack position to finish the clean.

Why: The kettlebell rack position isn’t quite a standalone exercise either—but it’s an essential technique for holding the implement for all types of exercises, from lunges to carries. Mastering the rack position is fundamental to other kettlebell moves because it forces you to control and stabilize the weight in many directions at once. Taming the kettlebell in this way develops the grip, core, and glute strength you’ll need when you start pressing, swinging, and cleaning these unruly implements as the workouts grow in complexity.

Key Performance Points: The interior portion of the forearm should be pressed against your chest, which solves incorrect elbow position (it should be pointing down). Do not straighten your wrist. It should be in a strong flexed position, which will take pressure off your elbow. Keep your shoulders packed down. Your shoulder blades should be pulled down and back as opposed to lifted up like you’re shrugging (which will overtax the upper trap).

Sets and Reps: Start with single-arm front racks, doing each arm individually. Clean the bell into place, and hold for 30 seconds. Do 3 sets per side.

Why: Here, you’re working to push the weight overhead. Pressing a kettlebell is fundamental and you can start by doing it from a standing position. Learning to properly overhead press the bell does a few things. First off, it teaches you to own the overhead press position, which will set you up for overhead carries, windmills, getups, and other moves that will make kettlebell training even more fun. It also builds shoulder stability and increases your core stability.

Key Performance Points: Start from a good rack position. Keep your wrist forward at the top. Keep your biceps close to your ear, and keep a solid foundation by flexing your lats and shoulder blades.

Sets and Reps: Do 3 sets of 8 to 10 reps per side. Rest minimally between each set.

Why: Finally, you’ll use a hip hinge to bring the kettlebell overhead. This is the biggest test of your ability to generate power from the floor, and to coordinate the action of your upper and lower body. Learn to do it well and other strength and athletic movements will feel easy by comparison.

Key Performance Points: Don’t swing the kettlebell too far out in front of you. Guide the kettlebell even closer to your body and higher with your high pull. Once it’s level with your head, you’re done pulling—now you’re punching, getting your hand up to that locked-out position.

Sets and Reps: Do 10 reps, switch arms, and repeat.

Why: The basic halo is an incredibly effective movement that builds shoulder stability while working the core. It strengthens your mind-muscle connection, requiring full concentration and a team effort from your shoulders, triceps, back, and core to maneuver the unwieldy weight around your head. As a result, you can increase focus, boost intensity, and get the most out of every single rep. There’s more. In most gym movements, the weight follows a linear path. The halo is what exercise physiologists call a “transverse plane”—or rotational—exercise, which forces you to initiate movement and stabilize all 360 degrees of your core muscles, much as you do in life and sport. Over time, transverse plane moves build a tougher, more resilient core that is able to withstand shocks and absorb impact from all directions.

Key Performance Points: Keep your abs tight! The halo is harder than you think, and very easily can become a hula dance, with your hips moving and your lower back arching. Offset this by tightening your abs to pull your ribcage in. Don’t rush. Work the bell around your head slowly.

Sets and Reps: Try the exercise for 10 to 20 reps per direction to start before adding extra challenges, like the kneeling position or a squat, for a bigger metabolic impact.

Why: This compound exercise isn't just for the barbell. You'll hone your hip hinge using a kettlebell to deadlift, which can be a great reason for a beginner to add the movement to their routine. You're working the same movement as the good morning—you'll just change the position of the load to pick it up from the floor (which winds up being much more practical, both in your training and IRL).

Key Performance Points: Don't look up. As you lift the weight from the floor, keep your gaze trained on a spot on the floor just in front of you. This will help to keep your neck in a neutral position, which is important for spine health.

Sets and Reps: Start with 8 to 12 reps. You won't be able to load up as heavily with kettlebells as other implements, so focus on mastering the movement pattern here.

Why: This exercise is one of the best ways for beginners to learn how to squat, period. The position of the load helps to reinforce proper posture without being too difficult to handle, reinforcing bracing and spinal stability in ways the back squat doesn't.

Key Performance Points: Don't sweat depth. You'll be in a good position to lower down to the right spot for you; just avoid stopping before you hit parallel.

Sets and Reps: Start with 8 to 12 reps. Like the deadlift, you won't be able to load up as heavily with kettlebells as other implements when you squat, so focus on mastering the movement pattern here.

Why: This might look like you're just walking in place, but the kettlebell march is actually a challenging core exercise. The key is in the position of the load and bracing your abs—which you'll quickly discover is much harder than it looks.

Key Performance Points: Don't rush. Your steps should be slow and deliberate as you fight to keep your hips square and the weight level.

Sets and Reps: You can march for reps or for time. Try for 30 seconds on, 30 seconds off for 3 minutes.

Why: You’re giving your abs a uniquely challenging movement here, addressing both the bracing and rotational aspects of your core. If that weren’t enough, holding the weight overhead is a sneaky-effective way to hone shoulder health, too.

Key Performance Points: Key your eyes locked on the weight overhead throughout the movement. Keep your ribcage tight, and move slowly with intention instead of jerking up and down.

Sets and Reps: Perform 3 sets of 6 to 8 reps per side, making sure to move slowly through each step.

Why: This is your opportunity to build power without as much technical challenge as the snatch. Even better, once you swap kettlebells for the barbell lots of people use for push presses, you’ll be putting your shoulders in a much healthier position.

Key Performance Points: You’re doing a push press, not a thruster. Don’t squat down; keep your depth shallow.

#4989. EU taps industry to set standards for tokenized securities

Applications close Nov. 27 for an industry group that aims to draft initial standards for holding and settling tokenized securities by early 2028.

The European Central Bank, European Commission and European Securities and Markets Authority are seeking nominations for an industry-led group that will draft common standards for holding and settling tokenized securities.

Eligible applicants include EU industry associations, global institutions, private firms and fintech companies. They can apply to the European Tokenisation Standards Group (ETSG) by Nov. 27, with work set to begin in January 2027, according to a release published Monday. The three EU institutions will participate as observers.

The group will develop technical and market standards to help companies hold and settle tokenized securities across different distributed ledger technology (DLT) platforms and ensure those platforms can work with existing financial systems. The group aims to produce an initial set of standards by early 2028, according to its mandate. The standards will support Appia, the Eurosystem’s initiative to develop a blueprint for Europe’s tokenized financial markets.

The institutions warned that fragmentation could limit the benefits of tokenization and said the group may recommend ways for EU authorities to encourage firms to adopt its standards.

In September, the Eurosystem launched Pontes, a system that allows financial institutions to settle wholesale transactions in tokenized assets in central bank money.

Related: ECB President Christine Lagarde to step down in February 2027

#4988. What Is It Supposed to Feel Like to Push Your Limits? 5-Time CrossFit Games Champ Mat Fraser Knows.

No pain, no gain? The GOAT shares his perspective.

MAT FRASER UNDERSTANDS what it’s like to push your limits. But the five-time CrossFit Games champ found the motivation to train to failure at the 2015 CrossFit Games, when he finished in second place. Fraser remembers feeling devastated—finishing as a runner-up felt like a personal offense to his purpose and identity.

“Once I made that commitment to get back in the gym and give it another shot, I kept that moment fresh,” Fraser says in the latest episode of Strong Talk. “Knowing that you don't get that beautiful moment in front of thousands of people without the thousands of moments by yourself in your gym when no one's patting you on the back and no one's cheering for you.”

What did it feel like to push his limits in his workouts? Fraser says he’s on the right track when he’s “tasting pennies,” the top of his head is tingling, and his heart rate is over 200 bpm. “Leave no doubt on the table, and be proud of myself. Then no matter what results I get, I know that's what I earned,” he explains.

But despite his preparation, Fraser is only human. Despite his impressive accolades, he never got rid of the pre-event jitters. Fraser tells MH fitness director Ebenezer Samuel, C.S.C.S. and exercise physiologist and strength coach Dr. Pat Davidson that he used to dry heave or grab a bucket as his breakfast came back up before every competition. In the episode, the trio delved more into the hardest workouts he did to prepare for the Games, what it's like to train recreationally versus for competition, and how to exercise and fuel your body when you only have 15 minutes.

#4987. Nvidia's $350 Billion Scare: What Happens If Jensen Huang Leaves?

Nvidia's $350 Billion Scare: What Happens If Jensen Huang Leaves?

Nvidia shares plunged as much as 8% Monday, briefly threatening to erase roughly $350 billion in market value as investors weighed the possibility that CEO Jensen Huang could lose his job over an alleged Chinese intelligence operation.

For a company that has become the face of the artificial intelligence boom, that's a rather expensive scandal. And the investigation is just getting started.

Nvidia's board opened an independent investigation following reports that Huang had a romantic relationship with a business executive whom U.S. intelligence believes was recruited by China's Ministry of State Security.

Huang acknowledged being unfaithful to his wife in a message to employees, apologized and pledged to cooperate. Nvidia's board said it had no reason to believe company secrets or U.S. national security had been compromised, and expressed confidence in his leadership.

The allegations have not established that Huang disclosed sensitive information or knowingly cooperated with foreign intelligence. Investors, however, are already confronting the possibility of a much bigger problem.

Nvidia's market capitalization had declined from roughly $5.78 trillion on October 6 to $4.51 trillion by November 6, according to YCharts. That's approximately $1.27 trillion in lost value before the board publicly announced its investigation.

The latest controversy adds another risk to a stock already under pressure from questions about the sustainability of massive AI infrastructure spending.

Nvidia also carries enough weight to move the broader market. A November 6 snapshot placed the company at 6.93% of the SPDR S&P 500 ETF, which tracks the market-capitalization-weighted index of major U.S. companies.

At that weight, a 5.6% decline in Nvidia alone would subtract approximately 0.39 percentage points from the S&P 500's performance, assuming everything else remained unchanged—which most likely will not happen, considering Nvidia is the main hardware provider for many other companies in the country, including some in the top 10.

Yet the S&P 500 rose 1.48% Monday morning, while the Nasdaq gained 1.87%. Other semiconductor stocks also rallied, suggesting investors were treating Nvidia's troubles as largely company-specific rather than abandoning the broader AI trade.

Huang has led Nvidia since co-founding it in 1993. Replacing him would involve considerably more than finding another executive capable of presenting a new GPU.

The 63-year-old CEO has built an unusually centralized management structure and maintains important relationships with customers, suppliers and governments. Analysts have questioned whether Nvidia has an obvious successor.

Chris Weston, head of research at Pepperstone, told Reuters he didn't expect Huang to resign. But if investors genuinely believed his departure was imminent, the stock's reaction could be an "absolute bloodbath."

Bloodbath or not, the stock would be exposed to a lot of uncertainty. After all, Huang is the man responsible for building a whole imperium. It would be similar to Mark Zuckerberg leaving Meta, Elon Musk leaving Tesla or Vitalik Buterin disowning Ethereum. Beyond pure market analysis, Huang is the name behind Nvidia and market sentiment can react to that.

Dan Ives of Yorkville Ives & Co. similarly described Huang as not replaceable, although he acknowledged the investigation could ultimately result in his removal.

An extramarital affair alone does not automatically justify dismissing a CEO. Unless the scandal becomes a reputational risk for Nvidia, it’s possible that Huang escapes the scandal and markets recover as long as the company continues being extremely profitable. The more consequential question is whether Huang's conduct exposed sensitive information, created security vulnerabilities, or compromised Nvidia's business interests.

So far, the company has announced no findings establishing such failures.

A resignation could trigger another sharp selloff, particularly without a credible succession plan. But there is no reliable way to calculate how far the stock would fall, or whether investors would ultimately welcome new management.

Nvidia has been in a sustained bullish trend ever since ChatGPT entered the market in 2023. That said, its weekly chart was already flashing warning signs before the allegations emerged.

The stock traded near $179.34 Monday, significantly below its 50-week exponential moving average of approximately $199.16. That indicator tracks the longer-term price trend while assigning greater weight to recent trading.

The breakdown is particularly significant because Nvidia had spent much of its recent bull market above that average. Its last comparable breach occurred during the 2025 correction and the pre-ChatGPT era before that in 2022-2023.

The stock had already crossed below that indicator before the scandal, meaning the investigation accelerated an existing bearish trend rather than causing the entire breakdown.

Based on the weekly chart, several levels stand out:

These are potential price targets based on technical analysis, and a recovery above $190, followed by a move back above the 50-week EMA near $199, would improve the technical outlook.

Monday's weekly candle also remains unfinished, so a confirmed weekly close below the indicator would carry more weight than an intraday move.

President Donald Trump has already defended Huang, describing him as an honorable person who has done an exceptional job for the United States.

"I think he probably has an excuse for it. You have to hear it," Trump told reporters Sunday.

The president's support could help Huang politically, but the controversy comes as Nvidia navigates Washington's restrictions on advanced AI chip exports to China.

Huang has repeatedly advocated allowing Nvidia to sell processors to Chinese customers. Allegations involving a suspected intelligence operation could complicate those negotiations, even without evidence that the CEO knowingly participated in espionage.

China's Foreign Ministry has rejected the allegations as baseless.

Congressional Republicans have also requested a private briefing on Nvidia's security procedures, with lawmakers seeking Huang's agreement to a briefing date by November 11, according to Axios.

Investors have another important date approaching. Nvidia is scheduled to report its quarterly earnings on November 17, giving the market an opportunity to assess its underlying business against the uncertainty surrounding its CEO.

The board has not disclosed when its independent investigation will conclude.

#4985. Renewables to reach 40% of total generating capacity, beat gas within a year – EIA

Over the coming year, renewables’ share of total generating capacity will grow from 37.7% to 40.4%, surpassing that of natural gas and outstripping nuclear capacity fivefold, according to new data released by the US Energy Information Administration (EIA) and reviewed by the SUN DAY Campaign.

According to the EIA’s latest “Electric Power Monthly” report (with data through August 31, 2026), renewably generated electricity during the first two-thirds of 2026 was 10.2% greater year over year. The growth was led by utility-scale [>1 MW] solar (up 20.0%), small-scale solar (up 12.0%), wind (up 7.8%), hydropower (up 4.2%), and geothermal (up 3.8%).

From January to August 2026, wind produced 333,367 gigawatt-hours (GWh) – 10.6% of total US electrical generation – while utility-scale and small-scale solar combined produced 329,119 GWh (10.5%), hydropower produced 180,940 GWh (5.7%), biomass produced 29,917 GWh (1.0%), and geothermal produced 10,766 GWh (0.34%).

By comparison, the electrical output of US natural gas and nuclear power plants grew more slowly – by just 2.6% and 1.3%, respectively. Electricity produced by US coal plants fell by 8.7%.

During the first eight months of 2026, the combination of wind and solar, including small-scale solar, accounted for almost 21.1% of domestic electricity production, with wind at 10.6% and solar at nearly 10.5%.

During the first two-thirds of this year, wind and solar combined produced 44.4% more electricity than coal and 24.8% more than nuclear.

Through the end of August, the mix of all renewables, including biomass, accounted for 28.1% of total US electrical generation – up from 26.2% a year earlier.

Between September 1, 2025, and August 31, 2026, the installed capacity of utility-scale solar increased by 27,141.7 MW, while that of small-scale solar and wind grew by 6,526.1 MW and 9,979.1 MW (including 800 MW of offshore wind), respectively. The combined capacity of all renewable energy sources, including hydropower, biomass, and geothermal, expanded by 43,497.9 MW.

In addition, utility-scale battery energy storage capacity increased by 17,586.9 MW.

By comparison, coal capacity fell by 2,703.4 MW, while nuclear capacity remained unchanged. However, natural gas capacity rose by 5,316.0 MW. Combined, net new nuclear and fossil fuel capacity totaled just 2,612.6 MW.

Thus, renewable energy’s capacity expansion was nearly 17 times greater than the net growth of fossil fuels and nuclear power combined.

The EIA projects that the rapid growth of renewables over the past year will accelerate in the coming year.

As of September 1, 2026, renewable energy’s share of total US utility-scale generating capacity was 34.5%. EIA projects this to grow to 37.3% by August 31, 2027. Utility-scale solar will add 44,603.1 MW, thereby expanding its share from 13.4% to 16.5%, while wind will grow by 8,312.3 MW (including 3,355.0 MW of offshore wind), increasing from 13.4% to 13.5%. The mix of other renewables (hydropower, biomass, and geothermal) will add 201.9 MW.

The combined net capacity growth of all utility-scale renewable energy sources over the 12-month period (53,117.3 MW) is almost 44% higher than that added over the previous 12 months (36,971.8 MW). It would bring renewables’ share of total US utility-scale generating capacity up to 37.3%.

Meanwhile, EIA projects no new nuclear generating capacity and a net decline of 1,997.0 MW in fossil fuel capacity.

The figures cited above don’t include small-scale solar. The estimated capacity of small-scale solar systems grew by 6,526.1 MW during the last year, bringing its total to 63,192.6 MW. EIA does not provide a forecast for small-scale solar capacity growth over the next 12 months, but the SUN DAY Campaign assumes it will roughly equal the 6,400 MW added on average over each of the past five years. Therefore, it is conservatively assumed that an additional 6,000 MW or more will come online in the coming year.

If small-scale solar increases by approximately 6,000 MW by September 1, 2027, it will bring renewable energy’s installed capacity to about 550,000 MW. By comparison, natural gas’ projected generating capacity would total 518,312.8 MW.

Solar power’s share alone would be more than one-fifth (20.7%) of total US generating capacity.

EIA foresees battery energy storage adding another 23,656.2 MW by September 1, 2027 – an increase of 43.7%, bringing the total up to 77,753.2 MW.

Thus, the combination of utility-scale renewable energy sources and battery energy storage will provide 76,773.5 MW of new clean energy capacity by late summer 2027. With the inclusion of small-scale solar, that figure could rise to 82.8 GW of new generating capacity.

Google CAISO to see what percent of the California grid is supplied by renewable energy on each and every day, typically more than 70% and often 100%., And they have 16 GW of battery storage, providing renewable electricity 24 seven to the California electrical grid.

“Each new issue of EIA’s ‘Electric Power Monthly’ reinforces the theme that the growth of solar, wind, and battery storage has become unstoppable,” noted the SUN DAY Campaign’s executive director Ken Bossong. “Meanwhile, the share of the nation’s electrical generation and generating capacity provided by fossil fuels and nuclear continues to shrink.”

Read more: EIA: 83 GW of renewables and storage coming in 12 months as fossil fuel capacity to fall

#4984. Bitcoin in Limbo as Stocks Rebound Ahead of US Inflation Report

Bitcoin in Limbo as Stocks Rebound Ahead of US Inflation Report

After a tumultuous week across traditional and digital asset markets, Bitcoin has returned to prices last seen in early August, when the cryptocurrency mounted an impressive 25% gain in a single month.

This time, though, $75,000 looks a bit different for crypto investors, now that BTC is on the way back down. So where does Bitcoin go next? Here’s what the charts look like.

U.S. stocks rebounded Monday as oil prices fell, with the S&P 500 up 1.9% and the Nasdaq Composite up 2.2% by 11:30 a.m. Eastern. Brent crude fell 4.3% to about $94 a barrel, giving the broader market a lift even as Nvidia shares slid in a move that spooked tech investors.

The Bureau of Labor Statistics is due to release the October consumer price index tomorrow. The report tracks what households pay for goods and services, and it lands after a stronger-than-expected jobs report pushed traders to price in roughly an 80% chance of a quarter-point Federal Reserve interest rate hike in December.

Bitcoin was up 0.7% at $74,559 after touching its lowest level in more than two months. The rebound followed a wave of forced selling in crypto derivatives, with a CoinGlass snapshot showing $1.2 billion in liquidations over 24 hours.

Exchanges liquidate positions when traders can no longer cover their losses with the collateral they’ve posted. About $1.04 billion of the total came from longs, or bets on higher prices. Bitcoin positions accounted for $324 million, nearly 87% of it in longs.

Bitcoin rallied from roughly $57,700 to $87,350 before sliding back toward $75,000. The coin is currently trading at $74,685 after an early dip to $73,667, with the week’s candle still open.

The 50-week exponential moving average, or EMA, sits near $78,250, above the 200-week EMA at about $69,790. Both averages give more weight to recent prices, with the shorter one responding faster.

Their order still favors the longer-term uptrend, though Bitcoin is trading below the faster average, and the gap has narrowed considerably, which increases the possibility of a death cross in the future.

The Relative Strength Index, or RSI, compares recent gains with recent losses on a scale of zero to 100. At 47.8, it’s close to the midpoint, with a slight tilt toward sellers and some distance from 30, the level traders typically call oversold.

The Average Directional Index, or ADX, measures how strongly prices are trending in either direction. The chart’s reading of 21.9 is below the commonly used 25 threshold for a strong trend, even though its accompanying directional indicators give buyers a slight edge.

On the way down, $74,400 is the first area where buyers could step in. Bitcoin has already traded through it this week, so a weekly close below it would put $72,550 in view, followed by the $69,000 area around the 200-week EMA.

On a rebound, the first area where sellers could stall the move is near $76,040, followed by the 50-week EMA around $78,250. A move through that average would put $80,360 back in play, with the prior rebound high near $87,350 beyond it.

#4983. Strategy buys 1,036 Bitcoin as BTC holdings climb to 850,364

Strategy bought $81.9 million in Bitcoin last week while spending $188.3 million repurchasing STRC preferred shares, according to its latest SEC filing.

Strategy bought 1,036 Bitcoin for $81.9 million last week, bringing its holdings to 850,364 BTC.

The company paid an average of $79,012 per BTC last week, according to its Monday filing with the US Securities and Exchange Commission.

Strategy said it paid a total of $64.16 billion for its Bitcoin holdings, at an average price of $75,456 per coin. Its holdings now account for roughly 4.05% of Bitcoin’s maximum 21 million supply.

Since the start of October, Strategy has spent about $1.06 billion buying back its STRC preferred shares, nearly five times the $219.7 million it spent on Bitcoin over the same period.

Strategy raised $283.8 million in net proceeds last week by selling about $2.05 million shares of tis common stock (MSTR) through its at-the-market offering program.

Strategy used $188.3 million of those proceeds to repurchase about 1.89 million STRC shares, following $208.2 million in buybacks the previous week. It spent $81.9 million on Bitcoin and allocated another $13.6 million to its USD Cash balance.

Related: Strategy shareholders approve daily dividends for four preferred stocks

Strategy’s separate US dollar reserve fell to $4.77 billion from $4.79 billion after the company drew $21.1 million to pay dividends on its preferred stock.

Its USD Cash balance rose about 10% to $146.2 million from $132.7 million.

The latest Bitcoin purchase follows Strategy’s acquisition of 811 BTC for $65.5 million the previous week.

Strategy’s common stock fell 3% to $125 in Monday’s premarket trading, while its STRC preferred shares edged down 0.06% to $99.7, according to Yahoo Finance.

#4981. Binance Hack Launderers Mint Elon Musk and Animal Meme Coins to Move Stolen Crypto

Binance Hack Launderers Mint Elon Musk and Animal Meme Coins to Move Stolen Crypto

North Korea’s hackers have found a novel use for the internet’s favorite joke tokens: laundering a slice of the record $6.4 billion stolen from Binance.

According to a report from blockchain analytics firm Elliptic, the launderers are creating their own worthless meme coins and using them to shuffle stolen funds through decentralized exchanges in a way that breaks the direct onchain link between the thief’s wallet and the one collecting the proceeds.

The launderers mint a brand-new token, pair it with a real asset like Near or a stablecoin in a liquidity pool, pay stolen funds into the pool through one set of wallets to buy the token, then sell it back through a separate set of wallets to withdraw clean-looking assets.

Because the pool sits in the middle, no stolen funds move directly from the buying wallet to the collecting one, and the receiving wallets look like ordinary traders cashing out a meme coin.

Elliptic, in research credited to co-founder Tom Robinson, said the launderers deployed the tactic on Tron and Near, with a close variant on Solana. The clearest example came on Near, where between Nov. 5 and 7 they created at least six home-made tokens, each paired with Near in its own pool.

The coins carried the usual meme-coin trappings: cartoon logos of a bow-wearing monkey, a flag-draped duck, a chimpanzee and a fox, one even featuring an “Elon 2028 President” image of Elon Musk, who has no connection to the tokens. The names ran from the crude (FUK) to a riff on dog coins (SINU).

Each token cost about 25 Near tokens, roughly $90, to create. All six followed the same arc: launched, traded heavily within hours using stolen NEAR, then abandoned, with the leftover liquidity pulled on Nov. 8.

In total, about 117,000 Near, or roughly $430,000, passed through the six tokens before much of it flowed into stablecoins or a cross-chain bridge.

The sums are modest so far, a reminder that the bulk of the haul is still parked onchain. Elliptic earlier found more than $3 billion of the stolen crypto had yet to be laundered, and that the launderers were spinning up a new wallet roughly every 25 seconds to move funds at machine speed.

Crucially, the meme-coin trick doesn’t actually hide the money. Elliptic stressed the trail remains fully traceable onchain; the maneuver only slows investigators down, buying time in what is essentially a race to move funds before they get flagged.

It’s one cheap, disposable layer among many that North Korea’s launderers stack together. Elliptic first observed the tactic last year while tracing the $1.5 billion Bybit theft, also attributed to the TraderTraitor group behind the Binance breach.

For compliance teams, the firm warned, funds arriving from a liquidity pool shouldn’t be assumed clean, especially where freshly minted tokens and a handful of trading wallets are involved.

#4980. Bosch’s popular e-bike motor just got upgraded with more power and less weight

Bosch has unveiled the latest generation of its popular Active Line Plus electric bicycle motor, bringing more power, less weight, and a significantly smaller footprint to one of the company’s most widely used drive systems.

The new motor is designed primarily for everyday riders, including commuters, recreational cyclists, and those who want a comfortable e-bike that can handle everything from city streets to weekend trail rides.

And while it isn’t Bosch’s most powerful motor, the upgrades are fairly substantial.

The new Active Line Plus now produces up to 600 watts of peak power and 60 Nm of torque, along with a maximum pedal assistance level of 340%. That’s enough to give riders a healthy boost when accelerating away from traffic lights or climbing hills, without necessarily turning their everyday commuter into an overpowered mountain bike.

Like its predecessor, the motor is designed for Class 1 electric bicycles in the US, providing pedal assistance up to 20 mph (32 km/h) without a throttle.

Remember, the Active Line motor is the drive system model that many e-bike companies use for their modestly performing e-bikes. It gives good power and control, combined with all the tech we’ve come to expect from Bosch, but it isn’t the motor e-bike makers use when they need an electric mountain bike to scale a steep mountainous trail.

Perhaps even more interesting than the increased power is what Bosch has done to the motor’s physical design.

The company developed a new two-stage gearbox that helps reduce the motor’s overall volume by 17% while shaving approximately 1.1 lb (0.5 kg) off its weight.

The new drive unit now tips the scales at around 6 lb (2.7 kg), making it easier for bicycle manufacturers to integrate into slimmer, more traditional-looking frames.

Bosch also says the new gearbox uses specially machined gears to make the motor virtually silent, while updated sensors and software provide smoother, more natural-feeling pedal assistance.

That’s particularly important for a motor like the Active Line Plus, where riders are often looking for an experience that feels like riding a regular bicycle, just with a bit of extra help.

The motor also supports Bosch’s eShift electronic shifting system, which can automatically manage gear changes for riders who prefer to focus on pedaling instead of shifting.

Bosch has additionally improved the motor’s sealing and bearings for better all-weather durability. The housing uses approximately 40% recycled aluminum, helping reduce the carbon footprint of its production.

Trek will be the first North American bicycle brand to introduce the new Active Line Plus motor, installing it on the company’s Marlin+ electric bike.

Bosch also announced several other additions to its e-bike ecosystem, including a new compact LED Controller that’s 65% smaller than its predecessor, an updated ConnectModule with Bluetooth-based theft tracking, and a new 720 Wh PowerTube battery weighing just 8.6 lb (3.9 kg).

There’s also a new Intuvia 200 display, offering a 2.4-inch grayscale screen for riders who prefer a traditional handlebar-mounted display.

While high-performance e-bike motors tend to grab the headlines with increasingly outrageous torque figures, there’s something to be said for improvements to the more practical end of the market – something for the riders looking for convenient and effective transportation instead of peak performance figures.

For the average commuter or recreational rider, a quieter, lighter motor with plenty of usable power is likely to make a bigger difference than squeezing another few dozen Newton-meters out of an already powerful drive system.

And with Bosch’s extensive network of bicycle manufacturing partners, we can expect to see this updated motor making its way into plenty of new electric bikes.

Fulton & Roark Cathedral of Ruins is a dark, sophisticated scent that’s perfect for the season.

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?
